## 5j (plan, rev 24): an untimed burst starts when its stream can take it

Status: **plan rev 25** (implementer, `impl-j3`, 2026-11-07), continuing rev 24 (kept as `test-plan.v24.md`; rev 23 as `test-plan.v23.md`; rev 22 as `test-plan.v22.md`). Base `work/n47s5` at `85dc4ac` (5j-1, 5j-2); 5j-3 at `bf3ebb5`. Marks: VERIFIED (`path:line` or a test that asserts it) or INFERRED.

**Changes in rev 27** (5j-5b and the rest of 5j-4 and 5j-5; implementer `impl-j5`, 2026-11-07; the orchestrator's rulings on 5j-3 applied):
- **Ruling 2 (overflow before booking), both Providers.** MockRadio: already in this order (`start_v` and `end` are computed at `lib.rs:1705-1706`, before `accept` at `:1715` and `book` at `:1760`); no change. UHD: `Clock::try_instant` (`core.rs`) gives the instant or `None`; `book_burst` (`control.rs`) computes the end with it and refuses `UR-21: the burst time overflows the transmit clock` before `accept` books anything, then calls `busy.book(end.flatten())`. The §5 residual of rev 25 is closed by this order; no test reaches an `i64` overflow in either rig, so none pins it (recorded).
- **UHD fake tests (5j-4, sequences 2 and 7).** `ur_21_s18_an_untimed_repeat_on_a_playing_repeat_is_refused_without_time_error` (refused as an overlap, no `TIME_ERROR`, one start-of-burst) and `ur_21_s19_untimed_switch_takes_its_timed_twin_boundary` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/tests/fake.rs`, after `ur_21_untimed_sends_are_each_played`). **Deviation (recorded):** the two Runs have different grid phases, so the test compares each boundary as root ticks from its own switch receipt, and asserts the two lie within one repeat period (1 000 samples, 200 root ticks each). The timed twin's instant is `DEVICE_LEAD_NS` after `now`: the untimed Kernel instant (now plus the Provider's lead) gives the same bound; with `DEVICE_LEAD_NS + DELIVERY_ALLOWANCE_NS` the twin's boundary was 2.6 ms later. 20 repeats pass. Sequence 1 on UHD is `ea_16_untimed_repeat_after_stop_starts_at_the_stop_region_end` (`uhd.rs`), and sequence 3 is the parity test; no `ur_21_s13_…` or `ur_21_s14_…` exists, and none is cited.

The only observable difference is the start-up refusal, which needs the placed start to exceed the Kernel's instant; that cannot happen while `min_command_lead` (`DEVICE_LEAD_NS + DELIVERY_ALLOWANCE_NS`) is at least the device lead (`profile.rs:21`). The Mock's row 119 is not equivalent: the placed start is floored at the sync sample, and its declared killer kills it (re-probed on this tree, `probes-j/j5/N47S5-119-recheck-mutant.log`). **For the orchestrator:** leave row 128 out (as here), or record it in `known-survivors.txt` with this reason.

### 0. Facts the plan rests on

- **VERIFIED.** A Session `Vocabulary` action with no `at` takes `earliest` = `session_earliest` (`crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/coordinator/pipeline.rs:959`, fn at `:1143`): the coordinator's instant plus the Provider's `min_command_lead`, or the running clock's origin when that is later (RS-19, `design/04-run-and-session.md:180`). The compiled burst carries that instant as its `at` and a coercion `ezsdr.action.at = "asap"` (`crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/session.rs:367-377`). The `at` a Provider receives is therefore a lower bound, not a requested instant.
- **VERIFIED.** Once compiled, nothing says the burst was untimed: `PendingAction::Action(Action::TxBurst { at, requested_at: None, … })` (`session.rs:401-409`). Admission (`coordinator/admission.rs:146-160`) sets `at` and `requested_at` from SC-23a (`stream/burst.rs:31-43`: an off-grid instant gives `requested_target = Some`). `dispatch` (`admission.rs:281-288`) builds the `Dispatched` record every Provider receives (`crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/module_api.rs:910-919`).
- **VERIFIED.** The record's `target` is the burst's applied start, and an untimed burst reports `late_by` absent: `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/v58.rs` (around lines 420–445).
- **VERIFIED.** MockRadio: `handle_tx_burst` (`crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/lib.rs:1574`); the switch is forced `OnTime` (`:1634`); `SendAsap` moves by `first_free(ordinary)` (`:1651`); the refusal is `accept` (`:1686`); `TIME_ERROR{refused}` is emitted only when `send_asap_late_by` is set (`:1693`).
- **VERIFIED.** UHD receipt: `book_burst` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/control.rs:391`); `decided` (`:457`); `first_free` only under `send_asap` (`:510-511`); `accept` (`:530`). UHD dispatch: `dispatch_start` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs:1014`): a `SendAsap` moves the head to `later_bound(current, front)` with `move_start` (`:1025-1030`), `refuse` = `TIME_ERROR{refused}` (`:988`), `report_send_asap` (`:993`).
- **VERIFIED.** `move_start` (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs:379-397`) tests `[new, f)` against the other chains and the Stop region, skipping the head itself, and only then removes the head. At dispatch the moved head's own chain sits at its old start while the move is tested, so the untimed placement must remove it first (M3).
- **VERIFIED.** The Python client sends `at` = the enclosing `aligned` instant or none (`python/ezsdr/session.py:470-494` for `repeat`, `:496-512` for `switch`). `Tx.switch` sends `at = None` outside `aligned`. `repeat` and `switch` never send a flag.
- **VERIFIED.** The verb `send` is `CompileRule::TxBurst { repeat: false, late_policy: DropAndFlag }` (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/lib.rs:222`). Python has no `send`; an untimed finite burst reaches the Kernel only through a raw `submit` (sequence 3's route).
- **INFERRED.** MockRadio has no lateness at dispatch: it decides at receipt and never hands ahead of its own decision (`design/09-mock-radio.md` MR-42, MR-17). Sequence 5 is UHD-only (Q9).
- **INFERRED.** For an untimed burst the Kernel's `at` is at least the Provider's `ordinary_k` in the normal case: the Kernel adds the full `min_command_lead`, the Provider adds the device lead, which is that lead less the delivery allowance (RM-14). Sequence 1's test pins it.

### 1. The representation: one field on the dispatched record

**Chosen:** `Dispatched.untimed: bool` (`crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/module_api.rs:910`), a **required** field (no serde default; Q3, invariant 39), `false` for every Action that is not a Session action with no `at`.

- **Set once, in the Kernel.** `session::Compiled` (`session.rs:284`) gets `untimed: bool`, set `true` in `compile`'s `Vocabulary` `None` branch (`session.rs:367`). `submit` (`pipeline.rs:1004-1006`) passes `compiled.untimed` to `admit_with` **for every item** (a new parameter, not an index test), so every item the action compiles to carries it. `AdmittedAction` (`admission.rs:12`) carries it, and `dispatch` copies it into `Dispatched` (`admission.rs:286`). `admit` (the Module path) and the Schedule path pass `false`.
- **M2, one writer for `requested_at`.** Admission step 7 (`admission.rs:158`) sets `requested_at` from `admit_burst_target` only for a timed Action; an untimed one gets `requested_at = None`. Rationale: the Kernel chose the instant, so an off-grid earliest instant is not a request (Q2). `at` is still the instant admission computes (`session_earliest`, unchanged).
- **Why one bool on `Dispatched`:** it is the one record every admitted Action passes through, so the property is general (orchestrator's rule): any Vocabulary may read it; this step gives it a meaning for `TxBurst` only. Rejected alternatives:
  - *A field on `Action::TxBurst`:* TxBurst-only, against the general rule; it also changes `action.v1.json` and every Action that carries `at`.
  - *Making `at` optional on `Action`:* every Provider and admission (SC-23a, KC-28's origin test) would change; a Provider would still need the earliest instant as its lower bound.
  - *Deriving "untimed" from `requested_at == None`:* ambiguous, since an on-grid timed burst also has `None`.
  - *Reading the log's coercion:* a Provider does not see the Session log.
- **Only a Vocabulary with no `at` is untimed (Q4).** `SetParameter` (RS-19: "records no coercion") and `Stop` stay `false`: no Provider reads the flag for them. A later step may give the flag a meaning for `start_rx` without a Kernel change.
- **Test constructors.** `Dispatched` is `#[non_exhaustive]`. `Dispatched::new` (feature `testing`) keeps its signature and sets `untimed: false`; `Dispatched::new_untimed` is added beside it. The `Held` struct in `tx.rs` gets `untimed: bool`; its literal sites pass `false` (the `Dispatched` path sets it from the record).
- **Schemas.** `schemas/dispatched.v1.json` gains `untimed` (required). `schemas/action.v1.json` does not change; the regeneration diff confirms it. Regenerate with `EZSDR_UPDATE_SCHEMAS=1`, keep `crates/ezsdr-kernel/tests/schema_freeze.rs` green, no version bump, no `SCHEMA_CHANGELOG` entry (AGENTS §6).

### 2. The rule

**One placement function (M1): `Bookings::place(from, len, now, join_by, horizon) -> Option<i64>`** in `staging.rs`. It is the only placement rule:

```
loop {
    k = first_free(from, len is some, now, join_by, horizon)?      // None: an open repeat holds the rest
    join = the chain that continues at k, if one is joinable at now (as accept)
    f = front(k, len, join is some)                                 // the full front, not the start
    match the accepted region the full front [k, f) meets (chains other than join, and the Stop region):
        none  => return k
        met   => from = that region's front, and loop              // jump past what it met
}
```

Each jump raises `from` (a met region's front is above `k`), so the loop ends. `place` returns `None` only through `first_free`'s open repeat.

- **Untimed at receipt (both Providers):** one function, `Bookings::book_untimed(&mut self, at, ordinary, len, now, join_by, horizon) -> Result<i64, Overlap>`: `from = max(at, ordinary)`, `k = place(from, …)`, then `accept(k, …)`, returned start. A failing `accept` maps to its `Overlap` (so the reason text is unchanged); the placement is expected to make it succeed (INFERRED; sequence 3's test pins it). Each Provider calls it after `prune(now_k)` and calls nothing else for an untimed burst: **the late policy is not consulted** (Q1), so `decide` is not called. Its record: `target` = `k`, `requested_target` = `None` (Q2, M2), `late` = `None` (Q5). `None` from `place`: refused with `… overlaps an accepted booking`, **no** `TIME_ERROR`, nothing booked (Q8).
- **Timed `SendAsap` family, F36 (both Providers, the `OnTime` decision only):** `Bookings::region_move(&self, at, len, now, join_by, horizon) -> Result<i64, Overlap>`: if `at`'s full front meets an accepted chain, return `Ok(at)` and let `accept` refuse it (today's text); if it meets only the Stop region, return `place(at, …)` (`None` → `Err(Overlap::Booking)`); otherwise `Ok(at)`. A moved burst gets `TIME_ERROR { send_asap, action }`, `late_by` = `k − at` in nanoseconds, the record's `requested_target = at` and the same `late_by` (S12's rule, F40). **One shared ceiling rescale** (as `front_late_by`, `tx.rs`) converts the samples to nanoseconds for both the TIME_ERROR and the record (minor).
- **Timed `DropAndFlag`:** unchanged: refused, `COMMAND_REJECTED`, no `TIME_ERROR`.
- **Late `SendAsap` (`decide` returns it; both Providers):** `place(ordinary, …)` replaces `first_free(ordinary, …)` (M1; `lib.rs:1651`, `control.rs:510-511`). **Behaviour change:** a moved burst whose moved region met a later booking used to be refused; now it is placed after that booking. Recorded here and in the spec text (§6, RM-16).
- **Switch (sequence 7):** unchanged. The switch is forced `OnTime`, and its boundary comes from the receipt (`repeat_boundary(origin, period, max(bound, target))`, RM-37, UR-38). `target ≤ bound`, so the flag changes nothing; the switch does not call `book_untimed` (rows 119, 128).

**At dispatch (UHD `dispatch_start`, `tx.rs:1014`).** An untimed head that is late there takes the bound as today: `bound = later_bound(current, front)`. Its start is `Bookings::move_untimed(old, bound, len, …)`:
- removes the head's own span (`remove_head`) first, so the head does not block itself (M3);
- calls `place(bound, len, now = i64::MAX, join_by, horizon = i64::MIN)`: **no hand-over horizon** (M3: the owner hands in start order, and a chain before the bound was handed before it), and **no joins**: nothing is received at dispatch, so a join point (a chain's end) is held (INFERRED: a join point's deadline has passed by dispatch);
- places the head after its joined pieces' front (M3's sequence, §3 sequence 5);
- on `None` (an open repeat holds the rest) restores the chains as they were and refuses with the overlap text, no `TIME_ERROR`; `held.open.late` and `requested_target` stay unset.

**An untimed head overrides every non-OnTime dispatch outcome** (minor): `SendAsap`, `Drop` and `PlanViolation` all take the placement above, with no `TIME_ERROR` and no drop (Q1). `Err` from placement stays a refusal.

**Timed bursts other than F36 are unchanged:** the late policy, the owner's `move_start` at dispatch (5b-3), the hand-over horizon (5h), the joined-piece move (D24), the S12 switch (5g).

**Principle (F36's list), one line per receipt-time refusal.** Device state → late policy (SendAsap) or refusal (DropAndFlag); conflict with an accepted booking → refusal; untimed → queued behind it, or refused only when no sample is free.

- Overlap with an accepted booking (RM-15, UR-21, MR-16): conflict → refusal. Keep.
- Stop region, timed `SendAsap` (and `OnTime`-family): device state → late policy: `region_move`. **Done here (F36).**
- Stop region, timed `DropAndFlag`: refusal. Keep.
- Stop region, untimed: queued (`place`). **Done here.**
- Hand-over horizon (`Overlap::Handed`, 5h): conflict with a chain the owner may hold → refusal. Keep.
- Joined-piece move (D24): conflict → refusal. Keep.
- Dispatch collision (5b-3): conflict → refusal. Keep; for an untimed head, `place` moves it past the collision (M3).
- S12 switch (5g): device state → late policy. Keep.
- Second pending switch: conflict → refusal. Keep.
- `RejectAtPlan` Stop-region refusal: a plan-time policy has no late path. Keep the refusal (Q7).

"Pure" = `crates/ezsdr-radio/tests/staging.rs`. "Mock" = `crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/tests/mock_radio.rs`. "UHD unit" = `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/{control,tx}.rs`. "UHD fake" = `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/tests/fake.rs`. "Parity" = `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/parity.rs`. "Session" = `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/repeat_switch.rs` (Mock), `uhd.rs` (UHD, FakeDevice). "Kernel" = `crates/ezsdr-kernel/tests/run_session.rs`.

A `DropAndFlag` untimed head is moved too (the override). | UHD unit `ur_21_s15_host_lateness_moves_an_untimed_burst_silently` (`tx.rs`, `dispatch_start`): a held head with `untimed = true`, its own chain at its old start, a booked chain at the bound. Mock: none (INFERRED, §0). | `held.k`, `TIME_ERROR` events, `held.open`, the burst's emitted samples. | `TIME_ERROR` emitted; the burst dropped under `DropAndFlag`; the head placed inside its own old chain (too far, or it blocks itself); the head moved onto P's region; a placement that uses the hand-over horizon. | 122, 123, 124, 125, 129, 130 |

Mock `mr_16_untimed_off_grid_earliest_has_no_requested_target` (record `requested_target` None). | The dispatched action; the record. | `requested_target` set to the earliest instant (a request the caller never made). | 111 |
| 9 | An untimed `DropAndFlag` burst at receipt, in a Stop region | Placed at `B`, like any untimed burst (the late policy is not consulted, Q1); no `TIME_ERROR`. | Mock `mr_16_untimed_drop_and_flag_is_placed_at_the_stop_region_end`. | Record `target`, `TIME_ERROR` events. | Refused or dropped, or a `TIME_ERROR{drop}`. | 120 |

**Sequence 1 detail (the Stop region is the untimed path).** `halt` (`staging.rs:284`) keeps a repeat that started before the Stop, with its front capped at the bound; a repeat that starts at or after it adds the region `[t, B)`. Either way `place` returns `B` for `y`. The pure test builds both shapes.

**Sequence 3 detail (what `place` does).** `place` tests the **full** front of the candidate, not its start: a booking whose region begins inside `[k, f)` moves `k` past its front. `first_free` alone would accept `k` (row 112's mutant).

### 4. Existing tests that change (the expectations, not only the names)

- `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/control.rs:966` `ur_21_an_untimed_send_is_on_time_after_its_delivery`: the `Dispatched` is built with `new_untimed`. Its 1 ms and 3 ms reads stay on time (handed, no `TIME_ERROR`). The 4 ms read, which is late today and dropped, is **placed**: no `TIME_ERROR`, handed to uhd-tx, `held.k` = the first free sample at or after `ordinary_k(receipt-based now, lead)`. Renamed `ur_21_an_untimed_send_is_placed_after_its_delivery`.
- `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/control.rs:1010` `ur_21_an_untimed_burst_before_the_clock_s_origin_is_on_time_at_the_origin`: built with `new_untimed` (the Kernel never stamps before the origin, so the test's `at = 0` is its stand-in). The 49 ms read is placed, no `TIME_ERROR`, `held.k = ordinary_k(read, lead)`. Renamed `ur_21_an_untimed_burst_before_the_clock_s_origin_is_placed`.
- `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/tests/fake.rs:2004` `ur_21_untimed_sends_are_each_played_or_flagged`: renamed `ur_21_untimed_sends_are_each_played` (`renames.txt`); asserts 20 bursts and zero `TIME_ERROR`, not "each a burst or a drop". **Clock mode (checked before the assertions, as the ruling asks):** the test runs on the UHD manual clock; if an implementer finds it on a real-time clock, it is rewritten on the manual clock in 5j-4.
- `python/tests/test_easy_api.py:634-643` `test_ea_16_a_repeat_right_after_stop_transmits`: the `@unittest.expectedFailure` decorator is removed (an expected failure that passes is an error in unittest). The comment's F36 line is rewritten to say the untimed repeat lands at the Stop region's end. Assertions: `later["action"]["at"] is None` (kept); no `radio.TIME_ERROR` in `manifest["events"]["delivered"]`; **`target ≥ B` and that it transmitted** (wall-clock rule: not `= B`, and not that `x` ended by stop).
- `python/tests/test_easy_api.py:597-614` `test_ea_16_aligned_gives_its_calls_one_instant`: the `sdr.sleep(0.02)` and the "The wait stays until …(F36)" comment are removed. `later` is untimed, so it is placed at `B` with no `TIME_ERROR`. The `sent` assertions are unchanged.
- **Rust Session tests (wall-clock rule):** a test on the Mock's simulated time or on UHD's manual clock may assert `= B`; a test on a real-time clock asserts `≥ B`.
- `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/v58.rs` (untimed burst at the origin, `late_by` None): expected to pass unchanged; the run confirms it.
- **The Python F36 test and the Mock's new behaviour land in the same sub-step** (5j-3). Un-decorating it before the Mock change would fail the Python suite.

### 6. Spec texts (each with a row in its Changes table)

Draft sentences (the Test lines name the new tests above):

- **RS-19** (`design/04-run-and-session.md:180`): "A Session action with no `at` is untimed: the Kernel marks the Action it dispatches (`Dispatched.untimed`, MA-14) and sets no requested instant. Each Vocabulary decides what the mark means; for a radio burst, RM-15 says." Changes row.
- **MA-14 / the `Dispatched` row** (`design/05-module-api.md:118-121`, `:169`): add the field `untimed: bool` (RS-19; `false` unless the Kernel chose the Action's instant). Changes row.
- **KC-28 step 6** (`design/06-kernel-coordinator.md:341`): the admitted items are dispatched with the entry's `untimed` mark, on every item of the action. Changes row.
- **RM-15** (`design/07-radio-model.md:161`): the principle sentence of the brief, verbatim: "A booking that cannot start at its requested sample because of device state follows its late policy; one that meets another accepted booking is refused; an untimed one is placed at the first free sample." Then: "An untimed booking starts at the first sample `max(at, ordinary)` from which no accepted region or Stop region holds it (`place`), never earlier, and carries no `TIME_ERROR`; it is refused only when an open repeat holds the rest of the stream. The late policy does not apply to it." Changes row.
- **RM-16** (`design/07-radio-model.md:165-166`): "A `SendAsap` booking whose start meets only the Stop region moves to the first sample `place` finds past it, with `TIME_ERROR { send_asap }`; a `DropAndFlag` one is refused; a booking meeting an accepted chain is refused either way. A late `SendAsap` booking is placed past every accepted region it meets." Changes row. The RM-16 Test line names `rm_16_a_timed_send_asap_moves_past_only_the_stop_region`, the Mock and UHD tests and the Python test. **Behaviour change recorded:** a moved late `SendAsap` burst is placed after a booking its moved region met (it used to be refused).
- **RM-14** (`:157`): one sentence: the untimed burst's placement is RM-15's, and its decision is not used. Changes row.
- **RM-12, RM-37** (`:142`, `:344`): RM-37 gets "an untimed switch takes the same boundary as a timed one whose `at` is the same instant" and nothing else. Changes row (RM-37 only).
- **MR-16** (`design/09-mock-radio.md:136-139`): the overlap bullet gains "(an untimed burst only when no sample is free, RM-15)". **MR-17** (`:143`): "an untimed burst is placed as RM-15 says; the late policy is not applied to it." Changes rows.
- **UR-21** (`design/18-uhd-radio.md:243`): receipt and dispatch placement of an untimed burst, with the dispatch sentence of §2; the F36 timed rule. **UR-38** (`:295`): no change. Changes row.
- **EA-16** (`design/16-easy-api.md`): the `tx_streams[k].repeat` row: an untimed repeat starts at the first sample the stream can take (RM-15), so after `stop()` the next untimed repeat starts at the end of the Stop's region; a timed one meeting only that region moves with `TIME_ERROR`. The `stop()` row: "after a transmit stream's `stop()` its next `repeat` transmits, at the end of the Stop's region if it is untimed (RM-15, RM-16)". The `aligned` row: "its calls are timed and keep their late policy". Its Test line names `test_ea_16_a_repeat_right_after_stop_transmits` (un-decorated) and the Mock/Session tests. Changes row.
- **KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md KL-36** (`:134`) and **KL-37** (`:141`): "a timed burst" in the symptom and the When; a sentence that an untimed burst is placed after the booking it meets (RM-15) and is not refused by them; the workaround stays for timed bursts; the evidence lists the new tests. Status stays "Ceiling". Each commit that changes the entry updates it (AGENTS §2). **Q6: no new entry.**
- `rules.py` (`plan/maintenance/tools/rules.py --base <pre-step commit>`) must pass for every changed `design/` file.

- **5j-4: the UHD Module** (receipt, `dispatch_start`, F36 timed, `Held.untimed`, the late `SendAsap` via `place`). The changed tests in `control.rs` and `fake.rs` (renamed as §4). Fake tests for sequences 1, 2, 3, 4 (UHD), 5, 7. Rows 121–131 and the re-anchors `-72`. Clippy: `rdev.py -- cargo clippy -p ezsdr-radio-uhd --features uhd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.
- **5j-5: parity, the Session tests on both Providers, the spec and KL texts.** `parity.rs` (the harness pushes an Action with `untimed` through a new `push_untimed`); `repeat_switch.rs` and `uhd.rs` Session tests; design texts with Changes rows; KL-36/37; `rules.py`.

Each sub-step: `cargo fmt --all` before reporting; the new tests run once; a test that asserts a wall-clock-dependent verdict is repeated (20 runs) only if its verdict can depend on timing (§4 rule); the rows run only through `remote_mutate.py` at the orchestrator's gate.

### 8. What 5j does not change

- The Kernel's `session_earliest`, the coercion, KC-28's origin test, SC-23a's `requested_at` for a timed Action.
- Timed bursts other than the F36 Stop-region case: the late policy, the owner's `move_start` at dispatch (5b-3), the hand-over horizon (5h), the joined-piece move (D24), the S12 switch (5g).
- The refusal texts (no new reason). Q8.

### 9. Open questions for the orchestrator (with my recommendation, and the ruling)

- **Q1. Is the late policy ignored for an untimed burst, at receipt and at dispatch?** Ruling: yes, `DropAndFlag` included, both places.
- **Q2. `requested_target` for an untimed burst.** Ruling: `None` (with M2).
- **Q3. Serialization of `untimed`.** Ruling: a required field, no serde default (invariant 39).
- **Q4. Which Session actions are untimed.** Ruling: only a Vocabulary with no `at`; `SetParameter` and `Stop` stay `false`.
- **Q5. `late_by` for the timed F36 move.** Ruling: `moved start − at` in nanoseconds, the same amount in the TIME_ERROR and the record, one shared ceiling rescale.
- **Q6. KL.** Ruling: no new entry; KL-36 and KL-37 are narrowed to timed bursts.
- **Q7. `RejectAtPlan` in a Stop region.** Ruling: keep the refusal.
- **Q8. A distinct reason for "no free sample".** Ruling: keep the overlap text.
- **Q9. Mock parity for sequence 5.** Ruling: a UHD-only test and a parity exception listed here.
- **Q10. Naming.** Ruling: the two labels "5j (F30)" and "5j (F34)" in the record become "5i" (the fix-round items of 5i) in this plan; the new rows keep the brief's "5j" name in their `name` field.

### 10. Claims I could not verify, or that are INFERRED

- The untimed placement never refuses after `place` (`accept` cannot then refuse): INFERRED; 5j-2's pure test pins it.
- The Kernel's `at` is ≥ `ordinary_k` in the normal case (§0): INFERRED; sequence 1's test pins it.
- No join is open at dispatch (§2): INFERRED; a booking can be received only at receipt, so a join deadline has passed by dispatch. 5j-4's tests pin it.
- That no untimed Session test in the Rust suite expects a refusal or a `TIME_ERROR` other than those listed in §4: checked by search of `start_repeat`/`send` with `at: None` (`repeat_switch.rs`, `v58.rs`, `uhd.rs`, `parity.rs`, `fake.rs:2118-2185`, `control.rs`); the implementer runs the whole suite to confirm and lists any surprise as a finding.
- The `dispatch_start` late judgement by `start_outcome` and `late_policy_front_outcome` (`tx.rs:1020-1022`) for an untimed head: VERIFIED by reading; its exact interaction with the head's own chain needs the test in 5j-4.
- The re-check's cases (a), (b), (c): not in the cache; the pure tests in §3 cover the three cases M1 names (see the Changes list).

Scope: the "Fix round 3" items of `selfrev/rulings-1.md` on `work/n47s5`: F39–F46, with F36 deferred by the orchestrator (2026-11-07: the owner is reconsidering whether an untimed burst should get TIME_ERROR at all). No F36 edit was made; its test and the `sleep(0.02)` stay as rev 20 left them. Each item's disposition is in §8's rev 21 bullet. Behaviour changes: F39/F40 (a switch moved by its late policy sets its burst record's `late_by` to the amount its TIME_ERROR reports, through `held.open.late`); F43 (`Bookings::accept` returns `Overlap::Booking` or `Overlap::Handed`, and the horizon refusal has its own reason on both Providers). F41, F44, F45 and F46 are text, tests, rows and one KNOWN_LIMITATIONS entry.

- Overlap with the Stop's region (RM-16; SendAsap and OnTime bookings): device state → should follow the late policy. **Not done here: deferred with F36, owner decision pending.** Today it is refused.

- Joined-piece move (D24, `move_start` of a late joined piece): conflict with the chain it joins → refusal. Keep.
- Dispatch collision (5b-3, `dispatch_start` → `move_start`): conflict → refusal. Keep.
- S12 switch (5g): device state (the owner's cursor is past the boundary) → late policy, already done. Keep.
- Second pending switch: conflict with an accepted switch → refusal. Keep.
- RejectAtPlan Stop-region refusal: the principle does not name it. **Flagged for the owner.**

## Changes in rev 18 (5i: D74 settled, a misreading; implementer 2026-11-07)

- **D74 settled: every sample is played (a misreading, not a loss).** Test `ur_23_a_lone_burst_plays_every_sample_and_its_end_packet_is_its_last_sample` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs`, `txp_rig`, manual clock, FakeDevice; lengths 32, 33, 40 booked at 5000 with nothing behind them, run past `J(5000 + len)`). It asserts the send log and FakeDevice's transmitted samples (`played(5000, len)`, one value per sample, 5000 to 5000 + len − 1). The log for the 40-sample case (`probes-i/d74-variants.log` and `probes-i/lone-burst-test.log`): `tx_send 0 n=39 at=1000000 sob=true eob=false`, then `tx_send 0 n=2 at=1007800 sob=false eob=true`. `1000000` is `instant(5000)` and `1007800` is `instant(5039)`: the end-of-burst packet starts at sample 5039, which is the held last sample, and its second sample (5040) is the zero pad. So the reading "two-sample EOB at 5040" does not hold: the packet starts at 5039. Where the 5040 came from is not recorded (the probe `probe_tmp_b_alone` was removed without a log). The same path closes the 33-sample burst at `instant(5032)` (`n=2`, eob), and the 32-sample burst goes whole in one `n=32 sob eob` send.
- **The close path.** A burst with nothing booked behind it is closed by `close_unjoined` (`tx.rs:523`) once `continuation_open` is false, which is when `now > join_by(e)` (`staging.rs:215`). The owner's close at `J(e)` and "the following booking's absence at the lead deadline" are one path, not two: both are that step. The test covers the close at `J(e) + 1`. Variants probed (`probes-i/d74-variants.log`): a plain `txp_give` with no record (same sends and samples as the booked path); a booking whose device time is 4 ms (the burst is 1 ms inside the device lead, so it is dropped as late and nothing is sent, the expected result, not a loss).
- **Cause, since when, fix: none (no loss).** No behaviour change. The test is kept as the regression test that pins the held-tail close (no mutation row: the brief asks only for the test, and §8's 5i step adds no row).
- **Deviations.** D75: the regression test is not probed by a mutation row, as the brief for 5i asks for the test only. The probe run used the same tree (`probes-i/d74-variants.log`, a temporary test removed afterwards).
- **End checks (5i).** `cargo fmt --all` (run); `rdev.py -- cargo test -p ezsdr-radio-uhd --features uhd --lib ur_23_a_lone_burst` (1 passed); the other end checks are in §8's 5i bullet.

## Changes in rev 17 (5h: the receipt rule against the hand-over horizon; implementer 2026-11-07)

- **Step 1, confirmed (not a stop).** The UHD diagnostic `ur_21_s5_b_handed_before_an_earlier_a_is_received` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs`, FakeDevice, Manual clock, unchanged rule) shows B(40) at 5000 handed at device time 0 (its first send `n=39`, `sob`, at 1 000 000 ticks on the stream's clock), while the owner's horizon at receipt 0 is `at_or_after(0 + 10 ms) = 10 000`: B is handable. A(40) at 2 000 received afterwards is accepted by the record and dropped as late at dispatch (`TIME_ERROR`, `outcome: drop`). The cause is therefore "B already handed or handable", and step 2 went ahead. Log: `probes-h/step1-today.log` (it holds the test result only; the send trace came from the same run's output).
- **Horizon, from the owner's hand-over code (D70).** `window_cut = clock.at_or_after(now + window)` (`tx.rs:443-444`); dispatch gates on `k < window_cut` (`tx.rs:485`) and caps the send at it (`tx.rs:614`). The brief's `ordinary_k(now) + window` is not the hand-over bound (`ordinary_k` is the device-lead point), so the rule uses `handover_horizon(now, W) = at_or_after(now + W)` (`staging.rs`), `W = radio.timing.tx_in_flight_ns`. Both Providers call it: UHD `control.rs` (`horizon`, with `receipt`), Mock `lib.rs` (with `now`).
- **The rule (D71).** `accept(start, len, now, join_by, horizon)` refuses a booking with `start < c.start` for a chain `c` that may be handed: `Bookings::may_be_handed(c, now, horizon, join_by)` = `c.start < horizon` and no joinable chain `x` (`x.join`, `x.end ≤ c.start`, `now ≤ join_by(x.end)`). `first_free(k, joins, now, join_by, horizon)` moves `k` past such a chain's front. The first version (only `c.start < horizon`) broke `s15_parity_s4b_three_bookings_b_continues_a_and_c_plays_on_time`: C (s + 100) is inside the horizon but cannot reach the device while A's device burst is joinable (the owner holds it until `J(e)`, `tx.rs:521-528`, `continuation_open`). The joinable-blocker term keeps S4b. §1.3 step 5 states the rule.
- **Tests.** Pure (`crates/ezsdr-radio/tests/staging.rs`): `rm_15_a_booking_before_a_chain_inside_the_hand_over_horizon_is_refused` (inside the horizon refused; a repeat too; one sample beyond accepted; a booking after the chain accepted; a Stop-cancelled chain ignored), `rm_15_a_chain_behind_an_open_join_is_not_yet_handed` (the joinable blocker, open and expired), `rm_16_first_free_moves_past_a_chain_inside_the_hand_over_horizon`. UHD: the step-1 test is now `ur_21_s5_b_handed_before_an_earlier_a_is_refused_at_receipt` (`COMMAND_REJECTED` for action 2, no `TIME_ERROR`, B plays 39 samples). Parity (`crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/parity.rs`): see R5 below. Every existing `accept`/`first_free` call in the pure tests passes `UNHANDED` (`i64::MIN`), so their expectations do not change.
- **The S6 refused tests (D72).** `ur_21_s6_send_asap_into_a_later_booking_is_refused` and `mr_16_s6_send_asap_into_a_later_booking_is_refused` keep their names and assertions (`TIME_ERROR{refused}` and `COMMAND_REJECTED` for the moved booking), but their geometry changes: with the earlier chains inside the horizon the new rule refuses or skips them before any move could meet a later booking (`first_free` skips past each handable chain), so the move reaches a later booking only when that booking is beyond the horizon. UHD: A at 9 960, B at 10 010, C at 1 000 SendAsap (move to 10 002, refused by B). Mock: A at 2 009 960, B at 2 010 010, C at 2 001 000 (horizon 2 010 000). `ur_21_s6_send_asap_moves_past_an_accepted_span` and `mr_16_s6_send_asap_moves_past_an_accepted_span` are unchanged and pass.
- **Parity (R5, D73).** `receipt_order_cases`' B-first flag is `b_first(case) = case.2[0] == 1`. `s15_parity_booking_rule_both_receipt_orders` runs the cases with A first; `s15_parity_b_first_accepted_booking_rule` (`parity.rs:1884`) runs the B-first ones and is un-ignored. The table's B-first flag is set for R5's five sequences (S1 k = 5; S2 end + 1 for L = 4, 10, 32; S4 at s + 42). Both Providers now refuse A in each (see R5).
- **Spec (§7).** RM-15 states the receipt rule with the horizon and the join exception (`design/07-radio-model.md`); MR-17's "no in-flight front" sentence names the horizon refusal; MR-16's refusal list and UR-21's receipt sentence restate it (`design/09-mock-radio.md`, `design/18-uhd-radio.md`), each with its change-table row. The test lines name the parity test and the UHD regression test. KL-36 is unchanged: its text covers bursts booked inside a finite burst's region, not the receipt order of two bookings, so the B-first case is not a listed limitation.
- **Rows (§5).** `N47S5-81` … `-84` (new, 5h): the horizon comparison (`c.start <= horizon`), the 'starts before' predicate reversed, `first_free`'s skip (`filter(|_| false)`), and the joinable blocker (removed). The fourth row is D71's. Each old `old` of the earlier 5a–5b rows that quotes an `accept`/`first_free`/`held_until` call or the region clash now quotes the call with `horizon` (rows `N47S5-12`, `-18`, `-19`, `-20`, `-21`, `-22`, `-63`; `-63`'s anchor is now `early || self.region…`). Probes: `probes-h/summary.txt` (`-81` … `-84`, each baseline PASS, mutant KILLED by its killer) and `probes-h/summary-old-rows.txt` (the seven re-anchored rows, each KILLED by its existing killer). The probe copies were outside the repository and are deleted.
- **Side finding (D74, not in scope, not fixed).** On FakeDevice at 1 MS/s, a lone 40-sample burst at 5000 transmits samples 5000–5038 and then a two-sample EOB packet at 5040: sample 5039 is never transmitted (probe `probe_tmp_b_alone`, removed; the cause is not traced: `send` and `close_device_burst`, `tx.rs` ~640–643 and ~844). It needs its own check before a bench run.
- **End checks.** `cargo fmt --all` (run); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` (all passed, 0 failed); `rdev.py -- cargo clippy -p ezsdr-radio-uhd --features uhd --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `rules.py --base HEAD` (0 problems); `chmod go+r` on the changed and new files; no `__pycache__` in the tree; no test process left running. The mutation gate is not run (orchestrator only).

## Changes in rev 16 (5g: a late switch follows its late policy; implementer 2026-11-07)

- **Done: the owner's decision (2026-11-07), S12 (UR-38).** A switch whose boundary the owner has handed past is no longer refused outright. Its late policy decides, as for any late burst (RM-15):
  - `SendAsapAndFlag` (what `start_repeat` compiles to): the record moves the boundary to the first `origin + k·N` at or after the owner's cursor, on the replaced repeat's grid (its origin and period, stored as `Switch.grid` at receipt; not the new waveform's grid). The switch installs there. The owner reports one `TIME_ERROR { cause: late, outcome: send_asap, action: <id>, late_by_ns }`, and the switch row keeps `boundary` (chosen at receipt) and `moved_to` (the moved boundary; `null` unless moved). Outcome `applied`.
  - `DropAndFlag`: refused with its id, as before (the kept case).
  - The move is made in the shared record, `Bookings::install(id, cursor_k, moves) -> Option<i64>` (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs`), under the streams lock. `Bookings::switch(id, grid, boundary, now_k)` takes the grid. A Stop's cancellation and `advance` read the moved boundary, because the record stores it.
  - MockRadio never hands ahead of its own decision, so it installs at receipt with `moves = false` and never moves a switch (RM-37, MR-42: the Mock writes `moved_to: null`).
- **Done, code.** `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs`: `install(&mut Held)` takes the returned boundary; a moved one sets `held.k` (the switch's first sample), emits the `TIME_ERROR`, and writes `moved_to` into the row. `TimeErrorPayload` (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/lib.rs`) gains `action: Option<ActionId>` (`serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")`), set only on this event; every other `TIME_ERROR` has `None`. `schemas/radio/time_error_payload.v1.json` is regenerated for it (`EZSDR_UPDATE_SCHEMAS=1`). `crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/lib.rs`: `record_switch` writes `boundary`, `moved_to: None`.
- **Done, tests.** UHD (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs`, manual clock, FakeDevice, the `SwitchRig` of 5d-U):
  - `ur_21_s12_send_asap_switch_handed_past_is_installed_at_the_next_boundary` (replaces `ur_21_s12_host_stall_past_the_boundary_is_refused`): the owner's cursor is past 15 000 at 8 ms; the new waveform starts exactly at the moved boundary, the old one plays whole up to it, no end-of-burst is sent at it; one `TIME_ERROR { send_asap, action: 2 }`; the row has `boundary` 15 000 and `moved_to` the moved boundary; `applied`.
  - `ur_21_s12_drop_and_flag_switch_handed_past_is_refused` (the renamed S12 case, same sequence; `DropAndFlag`): refused with its id, the old repeat plays on, `moved_to` null. Renamed in `renames.txt`.
  - `ur_23_stop_before_the_moved_boundary_cancels_the_switch` (new): the owner takes a late `SendAsap` switch at a cursor of 19 500 (9.5 ms; the old repeat is handed in 2 500-sample pieces), so it moves to 20 000; a Stop at 9.5 ms bounds the stream at 19 503, before the moved boundary: `cancelled`, no new sample sent. The Stop reaches the moved boundary only because the record keeps it (row 77).
- **Done, pure.** `crates/ezsdr-radio/tests/staging.rs`: `rm_37_a_late_switch_moves_to_the_next_boundary_of_its_grid_and_a_stop_reads_the_moved_one`: a cursor on the grid moves nothing (`install` returns the chosen boundary), a cursor between two boundaries moves to the later one, `moves = false` refuses, a Stop bounded before the moved boundary cancels and one bounded after it applies.
- **Rows** (`plan/maintenance/tools/mutations.json`, text edits only, each `old` confirmed once by `anchor.apply`): N47S5-38 re-anchored (the new `record_switch` arguments); N47S5-42 retargeted to the DropAndFlag test (its `if true` mutant now targets the refusal); N47S5-46 re-anchored to `install(&mut held)`; N47S5-41 unchanged. New rows N47S5-74 … 80: 74 (the move keeps the chosen boundary), 75 (the move goes to the first boundary after the cursor), 76 (the policy branch: a DropAndFlag switch is moved anyway), 77 (the installed record does not store the moved boundary, so a Stop reads the chosen one), 78 (the `TIME_ERROR` does not name its action), 79 (the row records no moved boundary), 80 (the Provider does not move `held.k`, so the switch installs at the chosen boundary). Probes: see §8.
- **End checks (5g).** `cargo fmt --all --check` (exit 0); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0); `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` (1 951 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored: the R5 test); `plan/maintenance/tools/remote/rdev.py -- cargo clippy -p ezsdr-radio-uhd --features uhd --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0). Then `rules.py` and the file-mode and tree checks, below.
- **Deviations.**
  - **D65, the `TIME_ERROR` names its action.** The brief asks for "`TIME_ERROR` (send_asap, with its id and the late amount)"; the payload had no id field. The field is added as an optional `action` (RM-22's payload shape, `schemas/radio/time_error_payload.v1.json` regenerated). Other `TIME_ERROR`s carry none. A Vocabulary change before the freeze, not a version bump (AGENTS §6).
  - **D66, the moved boundary stays on the replaced repeat's grid.** The new waveform's period is not the grid it moves on: a switch that changes length still moves on the old repeat's origin and period, because the old repeat is what the cursor has passed (RM-37's `repeat_boundary` formula with the grid of the repeat it replaces).
  - **D67, `moved_to` is written from `install`'s return.** The record's settlement carries the outcome only (`take_settled`), so `tx.rs` writes `moved_to` into the row from `install`'s return value, in the same call. A reviewer may ask for `moved_to` to travel with the settlement instead; it would change `take_settled`'s tuple and about a dozen assertions. Not done in 5g.
  - **D68, the Stop test uses a cursor off the period grid.** The moved boundary is the first grid point at or after the cursor, so on the grid it equals the cursor, which the owner has already handed. A Stop bounded before it is then not reached in this rig (INFERRED: the rig's Stop bound is the device time plus one window, and the test shows the off-grid case only). The test uses a repeat handed in 2 500-sample pieces, cursor 19 500 → 20 000, Stop at 9.5 ms → bound 19 503.
  - **D69, the probe classifier.** The probe's first version classified every failing mutant as a compile error (`error: test failed` matched `^error:`), so its summary read COMPILE-ERROR for all eleven rows. The classifier is fixed (it matches `error[E…]:` and `error: could not compile` only), and the verdicts were read from the logs instead of re-running: no mutant log has a compile error, and each ends `test result: FAILED. 0 passed; 1 failed` (the filter selects the killer alone). `probes-g/summary.txt` carries the corrected verdicts.

## Changes in rev 15 (5f: D60, the parity remainder, leftovers, D45; implementer 2026-11-07)

The Kernel's compile of params into `TxBurst.metadata` (`crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/session.rs`) is unchanged. D60 of rev 14 is closed.

N47S5-50's `old` text names `"start_repeat"`, the verb it guards. N47S5-47's name no longer says the verb carries the metadata. The renamed filters of N47S4-37 (`mutations.json`) and R07 (`plan/phase7/tools/mutations.json`) follow the renames.
- **Done, item 2: the parity remainder (§4.6).** `s15_parity_booking_rule_both_receipt_orders` (`crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/parity.rs:1874`, active, passes): S1–S5 and S4c on both Providers in both receipt orders, on one two-channel transmit stream (m10). Its B-first accepted cases are in `s15_parity_b_first_accepted_booking_rule` (`:1884`, `#[ignore]`, #79 open): it fails on exactly the five sequences listed under R5 below. `s15_parity_switch_starts_on_the_old_repeats_end` (`:2033`, S8, D57, passes): each Provider's switched burst starts where its old repeat ends; on UHD, `device.transmitted` at `tx_device_tick(boundary - 1)` gives the old waveform, and the new one from the boundary. `s15_parity_provider_stop_cancels_a_pending_switch` (`:2070`, S11 and D58, passes): a Stop at 23 ms cancels the switch on both Providers (`cancelled`, one burst, no tone sent). Helpers: `tx_device_tick`, the `tone` fixture (500 samples of `[0.75, -0.25]`), `receipt_order_cases`, `receipt_order_mismatches`, `b_first_accepted`.

Renames, applied in `tx.rs`, the two `mutations.json` files, `design/18-uhd-radio.md` and `renames.txt` (the old names remain in the historical records under `plan/phase8/reviews/`, which are not edited): `ur_25_an_end_booked_after_the_first_read_cancels_the_held_burst_and_releases_its_reservation` → `ur_25_an_end_booked_after_the_first_read_cancels_the_held_burst_at_the_origin`; `ur_23_a_clock_end_cancels_a_held_burst_and_releases_its_reservation` → `ur_23_a_clock_end_cancels_every_held_burst`; `ur_25_a_burst_on_a_clock_that_never_started_is_cancelled_and_releases_its_reservation` → `ur_25_a_burst_booked_on_an_ended_clock_is_cancelled_when_received`. KL-13 and KL-15 (`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md`) checked against the code: no preemption and no stale value-wait text, so no edit. Dead code: `later_bound`, `late_policy_front_outcome`, `handed_end_before` and `merge_front` each have production callers (grep of `crates/`), so none is deleted.
- **Done, item 4: D45, diagnosis only (no fix).** Finding: a harness setup difference, not a Mock defect against MR-20, and not a helper defect. Probe (`probes-f/d45-probe.log`; a throwaway copy `/tmp/opencode/d45` with one extra test, `d45_probe`; the harness unchanged), each row `Harness::new(profile, …)` with a `device_lost` fault at 2 s:

  | profile | start | step at 2 s | step at 4 s |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `ideal` | none | Ok | Ok |
  | `ideal` | 0 | DeviceLost | Ok |
  | `ideal` | 2 s | Ok | DeviceLost |
  | `x3xx` | none | Ok | Ok |
  | `x3xx` | 0 | refused by MR-11 (the profile needs a 2 s lead) | Ok |
  | `x3xx` | 2 s | Ok | DeviceLost |

  The Mock fires a fault at `T0 + at_ns` (MR-20, `design/09-mock-radio.md:156`): `start` offsets the fault ticks by `T0` (`crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/lib.rs:2260-2281`), and a fault fires only once the device has started (`lib.rs:1880`, `lib.rs:2381`). The `x3xx` case of the D45 test never had a valid `T0` (unstarted, or started where MR-11 refuses, `lib.rs:2236-2243`), while `ideal` in the same test was started at 0; with a valid start the `x3xx` fault fires at 4 s, as `ideal` does. Nothing changed. An MR-20 test for `x3xx` (start at 2 s, loss at 4 s) would close the gap in coverage; it is not written, and it is the orchestrator's call.

- **R5: the #79 divergences and the parity test that shows each (both Providers, both receipt orders).**
  1. The original 1–3-sample case (S1): k = 1…4 agree in both orders (the active test). **Still diverges:** k = 5 with B received first (`S1 k=5 [B first]`: Mock plays [1, 2] samples, UHD [2]).
  2. S2 (S3's (a)), the end of A (L = 4, 10, 32): both orders agree (the active test). **Still diverges:** B at end + 1 with B received first, for L = 4, 10 and 32 (Mock plays [1, L], UHD [1]).
  3. S3 (b): n = 1, 20 and 39 agree (the active test). Sampled, not every n in 1…39.
  4. B received before A: `s5_parity_b_received_before_a_refuses_a` (`parity.rs:1737`) agrees. The B-first accepted cases diverge: items 1, 2 and 5 of this list.
  5. The contiguous booking after a long held burst (S4): S4 at 40 and 41, and S4c, agree in both orders (the active test); `s15_parity_s4b_three_bookings_b_continues_a_and_c_plays_on_time` (`:1917`) agrees. **Still diverges:** S4 at s + 42 (past the gap) with B first (Mock plays [10, 40], UHD [10]).
  6. S13: `s13_parity_b_refused_at_receipt_and_the_value_answers` (`:1899`) agrees.

  **Rev 17 (5h) closes R5's list.** Each of the five B-first sequences is now refused at receipt on both Providers, so A is refused and B plays: S1 k = 5 (B first), S2 end + 1 for L = 4, 10, 32, and S4 at s + 42 are each checked by `s15_parity_b_first_accepted_booking_rule` (un-ignored, passes). The UHD regression form of the step-1 test (B(40) at 5000 before A(40) at 2000) is `ur_21_s5_b_handed_before_an_earlier_a_is_refused_at_receipt`; the pure rule is `rm_15_a_booking_before_a_chain_inside_the_hand_over_horizon_is_refused`. S1 k = 5 and the other two sequences are covered on both Providers by the parity test only. The dispatch-time cause recorded below is no longer reached for these sequences (A is refused before dispatch); it is not separately fixed.

  Five sequences diverge, all with B received first and both accepted (1 + 3 + 1; `s15_parity_b_first_accepted_booking_rule`). [Rev 15 state; superseded by rev 17.] The cause is located, not fixed (the brief allows no behaviour change): UHD's `dispatch_start` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs`, ~lines 981–989) judges the earlier-starting A late by 10 µs against `self.chain.front()` (`late_policy_front_outcome`), so it drops A (`TIME_ERROR`, `cause: late`, `outcome: drop`); the Mock plays both. Whether the rig or the Module causes the 10 µs lag is not established (INFERRED). #79 stays open.

Each Provider's switch is checked against its own old repeat's end, not a shared boundary, because the per-Provider lead (RM-37) and the rig's receipt timing can move one Provider's boundary by one period.
  - **S11:** the Provider-stop test stops at 23 ms, not 25 ms: at 25 ms the Mock's Orderly stop applied the switch (boundary ~36 ms), so the test would test the other path.
  - **D58 at Provider level:** the test calls `radio.stop`, not `Tx::shutdown()` alone. `Tx::shutdown()` without a prior `end` leaves a pending switch `pending`, but every real path books `end` first (`Provider::stop` → `streams.end`, `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/mod.rs` ~694–715; cleanup → stop). The UR-38 text states the order.
  - **R5 is not done for five sequences.** They stay in `s15_parity_b_first_accepted_booking_rule` (`#[ignore]`), and #79 stays open for them. Their fix is out of this brief's scope. (Superseded by rev 17, 5h.)

## Changes in rev 14 (5e: the Session verb, Python `switch`, the second repeat, the event comment; implementer 2026-11-07)

- **Done.** The verb `switch_repeat` in the radio Vocabulary (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/lib.rs`, appended after `start_rx`: `CompileRule::TxBurst { repeat: true, late_policy: send_asap_and_flag }`). Its params reach the Provider as the burst's metadata (the Kernel's existing compile, `session.rs`), so the mark is the client's parameter: see D60.
- **Done.** Python (`python/ezsdr/session.py`): `Tx.switch(x)` submits `radio.switch_repeat` on `<radio>/tx/s<k>` with `params = {"radio.repeat_operation": "switch"}`, `at` from an enclosing `aligned` block or none, and no value; `_check_channels` (new on `Tx`) is the channel check that `repeat` and `switch` share; `_cf32` refuses a non-finite sample with `Error` before sending (D61). `repeat`'s docstring says a second `repeat` on a playing stream is refused.
- **Done (spec).** `design/16-easy-api.md` EA-16: the `switch(x)` row; the repeat row says a second `repeat` on a playing stream is refused and raises `Rejected` from the submit reply; the EA-16 test line names the five new tests. `design/07-radio-model.md` RM-12: `switch_repeat` added to the verb list and to the Test line, and the `rm_12` row says four verbs. `crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/event.rs` (`Action::TxBurst`): the stale "`metadata` is empty" comment now says "empty or the repeat-switch mark (RM-13, RM-37)". `schemas/action.v1.json` and `schemas/dispatched.v1.json` regenerated (`EZSDR_UPDATE_SCHEMAS=1`; the diff is that description only; no version, no `SCHEMA_CHANGELOG` entry).
- **Tests, Rust.** `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/repeat_switch.rs` (new file; the plan named none): `ea_16_switch_repeat_verb_compiles_to_the_marked_burst` plays a repeat on the x3xx Mock, submits `switch_repeat` with the mark (the `switches` section has one row, `applied`, no `COMMAND_REJECTED`), and with no params (no switch row, one `COMMAND_REJECTED`). `crates/ezsdr-radio/tests/radio_model.rs`: `rm_12_the_verbs_compile_as_declared` counts four verbs and checks `switch_repeat`.
- **Tests, Python.** `python/tests/test_easy_api.py`, changed by subject: `test_ea_16_repeat_uses_stream_membership_without_setting_it` (`:728`): the second call is `switch(ramp(500))`, after `sleep(0.01)`; asserts the verbs `start_repeat`, `switch_repeat` and the switch's only param, the mark. `test_ea_16_aligned_gives_its_calls_one_instant` (`:597`): the later repeat follows `tx.stop()` and `sleep(0.02)` (the in-flight window, simulated). New, mocked (class `StartValues`, the `session()` helper): `test_ea_16_switch_sends_a_marked_repeat`, `test_ea_16_switch_refuses_non_finite_and_wrong_channels_before_sending` (NaN, infinity, two channels on one), `test_ea_16_second_plain_repeat_on_a_playing_stream_raises_rejected` (the second submit's reply carries `refused`; `Rejected` with its event). The grep for other two-repeat tests found none that fail: the suite's only two failures were these two.
- **Rows** (`plan/maintenance/tools/mutations.json`, text edits only; `rules.py --base HEAD` → 0): N47S5-47 (`{"radio.repeat_operation": "switch"}` → `{}`, Python; killer `test_easy_api.StartValues.test_ea_16_switch_sends_a_marked_repeat`); N47S5-48 (`if refused:` → `if False:`, Python; killer `…test_ea_16_second_plain_repeat_on_a_playing_stream_raises_rejected`); N47S5-49 (`metadata: params.clone(),` → `metadata: Default::default(),` in `crates/ezsdr-kernel/src/session.rs`, the Kernel's compile of a verb; killer `ea_16_switch_repeat_verb_compiles_to_the_marked_burst`, cargo, `repeat_switch`); N47S5-50 (the switch's `self._check_channels(channels)` call removed; killer `…test_ea_16_switch_refuses_non_finite_and_wrong_channels_before_sending`); **N47S5-73** (new: `if not np.isfinite(array).all():` → `if False:` in `_cf32`; same killer).
- **Probes** (`probes-e/`; throwaway copy `/tmp/opencode/probe-e`, `probe-e.py`; each killer PASSes unmutated first, then the mutant alone): N47S5-47 KILLED (`{} != {'radio.repeat_operation': 'switch'}`), -48 KILLED (`Rejected not raised`), -49 KILLED (the marked case's assertion), -50 KILLED (`Error not raised`), -73 KILLED (`Error not raised`). P537 (the channel check of `repeat`, now in `_check_channels`) KILLED. Logs in `probes-e/`; `summary.txt` has the last run, `summary-first.txt` the first (the helper refactor came between them; 47, 48 and 49 were re-probed after it, and 50, 73 and P537 with it).
- **Deviations.**
  - **D60, the mark is the client's parameter (for the orchestrator).** The plan has the verb compile to the marked burst by itself (row 49's "the verb's declaration compiles without the mark"). That needs `CompileRule::TxBurst` to carry metadata, a Kernel change this stage excludes. So the Session passes the verb's params as the burst's metadata (its existing rule), and `Tx.switch` sends the mark. A client that sends `switch_repeat` with no mark gets a plain repeat, which a playing stream refuses (the acceptance test's second case). Row 49 therefore mutates the Kernel's compile (`session.rs`: the params dropped) and not the declaration. **Decision needed:** whether the Vocabulary should own the mark (a `CompileRule` field, a Kernel change) before the freeze, or the client-side mark stays (the design of Sol 6, "no Kernel metadata change").
  - **D61, the finite check is in `_cf32`, not only in `switch`.** The brief says `switch` checks "as `repeat`: channel count, finite samples", but `repeat` checked no finite samples (its NaN was refused by the Provider). `_cf32` (shared by `repeat`, `switch` and `waveform()`) now refuses a non-finite sample with `Error` before sending; a NaN `repeat` is now a client `Error`, not a Provider refusal (both send nothing). Row 73 is its row; the plan had none (rows 47–50 only).
  - **D62, the channel check is a helper.** `repeat` and `switch` would have held the same three lines (a duplicated decision, §9 maintainability); `Tx._check_channels` holds them and both call it. Row P537's text is now in the helper (killed as before). `switch`'s target is written `f"{self._direction}/s{self._number}"` (as `_Stream.stop` writes it), because the literal `f"tx/s{self._number}"` of `repeat` is anchored by row P542 and the two would match twice.
  - **D63, no other file.** The Rust test is in `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/repeat_switch.rs` (new): the plan named no file, and the Session harness (`loopback_run`, `end_session`) lives in `ezsdr-acceptance`.
  - **D64, a schema description changed.** The `event.rs` comment is rendered into `schemas/action.v1.json` and `dispatched.v1.json`: regenerated (description-only; `ov_22_schema_freeze` failed before the regeneration and passes after).
- **End checks (rev 14).** `cargo fmt --all` (no change outside the edited files); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 1 945 passed, 0 failed (rev 13 had 1 944; the new acceptance test); `rdev.py -- cargo clippy -p ezsdr-radio-uhd --features uhd --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `rules.py --base HEAD` → 0; the Python suite on 3.14 (`~/.cache/ezsdr-venv`, `EZSDR_SERVER` at the server built from this tree): 105 OK; on 3.9 (`python:3.9-slim`, the gate's command): 105 OK. `chmod go+r` on the changed and new files, and no `__pycache__` in the tree. Not run: the mutation gate (only the orchestrator runs it).

## Changes in rev 13 (5d-U: the repeat switch on the UHD Module, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **Done.** `crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs`: `Switch` gains `installed`; `Bookings::install(id, cursor_k)` takes the pending switch while `cursor_k ≤ boundary` and otherwise settles it `Refused` (outcome `refused_past_boundary`); `cancel_switch()` settles a pending switch `Cancelled` (the F8 and loss paths); `advance` and `halt` apply only installed switches (an uninstalled one is cancelled by `halt`); `SwitchRecord` moved here from the Mock, with `SwitchOutcome::label()`. The Mock calls `install(id, now_k)` after `switch` (always taken at receipt).
- **Done.** `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/`: `control.rs` `book_burst` takes the switch when `repeat` and the mark (`is_switch`); the metadata rule is "empty, or the mark on a repeat" (row 45); a switch skips the policy judgement (never late, as the Mock's); its boundary is `repeat_boundary` of the stream's `Playing` (origin, period, UHD's D50 state, set on every accepted repeat); `busy.switch` refuses a second pending switch or a non-playing stream with the id; the `switches` row is written at receipt with `pending`. `tx.rs`: `Held.switch`; the switch reaches the owner as `TxCmd::Burst` and `command` calls `install` with the handed cursor (`open.next`, else `chain.next()`), refusing it with its id (`COMMAND_REJECTED`, UR-21) when the cursor has passed the boundary; `step` calls `advance` at device time; `join_open` takes a switch without a policy decision (m9: the held cut is a continuation, no end-of-burst at `b`, the existing `held_cut` path); `halt_failed_reopen` cancels a pending switch (F8). `core.rs`: `Playing`, `StreamState.playing`, `Records.switches`, `Core::settle_switches()` (called from `write_sections`); `mod.rs` writes the `switches` section.
- **Done (spec).** `design/18-uhd-radio.md`: UR-36 keeps the record of the pending switch and the repeat it replaces (one sentence, its Test line adds two tests); new UR-38 (the next free ID) cites RM-37 with its `*Test:*` line; Changes row for 2026-11-07. `rules.py --base HEAD` → 0.

At 7 ms the cursor stands on 15 000, nothing of the new waveform is handed, and the Stop's zeros start there), `ur_23_provider_stop_and_loss_cancel_the_pending_switch` (the record's `end`, with and without loss), `ur_22_f8_recovery_that_ends_the_repeat_cancels_the_pending_switch` (a reopen fault: `cancelled`), `ur_21_metadata_is_empty_or_the_mark_only` (other metadata refused; the mark on a stream with no repeat refused; empty accepted), `ur_21_s9_switch_different_length_sets_the_new_period` (500-sample switch, then a 300-sample one whose record row names 500 as its old period). The prepared waveforms gain 500 and 1 000 samples.

Two existing rows were re-anchored with the same mutant program: N47S5-35 (its `old` now reads `s.installed && s.boundary <= now_k)`, since `advance` checks `installed`) and the P1205 anchor is unchanged (the new F8 cancel line sits above `streams.finished(key)`).
- **Probes** (`probes-du/`; throwaway copy `/tmp/opencode/probe-du`, unmutated baseline PASS first, one row at a time, `probe.py` and the logs there): N47S5-41 KILLED (`ur_21_s8_switch_at_the_exact_handed_cursor_installs`); -42 KILLED (`ur_21_s12_host_stall_past_the_boundary_is_refused`); -43 KILLED (`ur_21_s5_second_switch_refused_while_device_time_is_before_the_boundary`), after the test gained its "pending" assertion: the first probe SURVIVED, because the refusal alone is the same with the early apply (D53); -44 KILLED (`ur_22_f8_recovery_that_ends_the_repeat_cancels_the_pending_switch`); -45 KILLED (`ur_21_metadata_is_empty_or_the_mark_only`); -46 KILLED (`ur_21_s8_switch_installed_before_the_boundary_sample`); -52 KILLED (`ur_23_switch_cut_reuses_held_cut_path`); -53 KILLED (`ur_21_s9_switch_different_length_sets_the_new_period`).
- **D51, install lives in `staging.rs`.** The plan put `install` in `tx.rs`. The record's decision (`cursor_k ≤ boundary`, the refusal and the settlement) is in `Bookings::install`, which the Mock also calls; `tx.rs` only supplies the cursor. Rows 41 and 42 are therefore anchored in `staging.rs` and their killers are the UHD tests.
- **D52, the switch reaches the owner as a burst.** Control's receipt books the record (`switch`, `pending`); the owner's `command` takes it (`install`), and a refusal there is asynchronous (Q8), reported with the switch's id as `COMMAND_REJECTED` (`UR-21: the repeat switch was not taken …`). Control has no cursor, so the owner is the only place the cursor rule can run.
- **D53, row 43's mutant is the early `advance` at the owner's step.** The plan's "pending cleared at hand-over" becomes `busy.advance(i64::MAX)` in `Tx::step`: the switch is applied at the owner's first step rather than at device time `b`. Its effect is visible only in the switch's outcome before `b`: the second switch is refused either way (the new chain has not started). The S5 test therefore asserts `pending` at 6 ms, which is the rule itself. Probed KILLED after the assertion was added.
- **D54, row 46's mutant is the skipped install, not an order change.** Structurally a switch's `Held` is inserted only after `install` (in `command`), so no sample can be handed before the install; an "install after the boundary sample" mutant has no place to live. The row removes the install call (`if held.switch && false`). Its killer checks that the switch is `applied`, which a skipped install never is.
- **D55, `halt` cancels an uninstalled switch whatever its boundary.** A Stop booked before the owner has taken a switch cancels it (the owner has handed nothing of the new waveform). Not tested: the owner drains commands before the Stop in every test here (INFERRED that the order is the run loop's; no test drives the other order).
- **D56, a switch is never judged late in UHD.** Both `book_burst` and `join_open` skip the policy decision for a switch, as the Mock does; the boundary is the only time it has. Covered by the S8 tests (their switches are booked well ahead), not by a test of a late switch.
- **D57, parity pins the records, not the samples.** `s15_parity_switch_to_a_new_length` compares the `switches` rows (S9 on both Providers); the S8 boundary sample is pinned per Provider (`ur_21_s8_…`). A device-level parity of the boundary sample is not written (the UHD rig's clock offset, S14's comment).
- **D58, shutdown is not tested.** §4.4 lists shutdown among the cancellations. The Provider's stop and loss reach the record through `end`, and no other path calls it; shutdown beyond `Provider::stop` is UNVERIFIED and not in the UR-38 text.
- **D59, parity names.** §4.6's `s15_parity_switch` is written as two tests (see Tests, parity) so that a failure names S9 or S10.

## Changes in rev 12 (5d-M: the repeat switch on the record and MockRadio, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **Done.** The switch in the shared record (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs`): `Bookings` gains `pending: Option<Switch>` (`Switch { id, boundary }`) and `settled` (drained by `take_settled`); `switch(id, boundary, now_k)` refuses a second pending switch and a switch with no repeat playing (a repeat that has started, has no end and has no front yet); `advance(now_k)` applies a pending switch whose boundary has been reached (the old chain's front is capped at the boundary, the new repeat's chain begins there); `prune` and `switch` run `advance` first (m3); `halt(t, stop)` settles a pending switch itself (applied when its boundary is before `stop`, cancelled otherwise); `is_switch(metadata)` is the mark test. The Mock (`crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/lib.rs`): `handle_tx_burst` takes the switch branch when `repeat && is_switch(metadata)`, refuses the metadata unless it is empty or the mark, chooses the boundary once at receipt (`repeat_boundary(origin, period, max(at, k(now + window + lead)))` with the playing repeat's origin and period, held in `TxStream.playing`), calls `busy.switch`, and writes the row of the new `switches` section (`SwitchRecord { action_id, predecessor, selected_at, old_period, new_period, outcome }`) at receipt with `pending`; `settle_switches` writes `applied` or `cancelled` once the record settles it. Stop, loss and `Provider::stop` reach it through `halt` and `settle_switches`.
- **Spec (done).** `design/07-radio-model.md`: RM-13 takes the mark (metadata "empty, or exactly the switch mark"; its Test line adds `mr_16_metadata_is_empty_or_the_mark_only`); RM-37 is the new switch rule (the next free ID: RM-36 is a withdrawn stub). `design/09-mock-radio.md`: MR-27 says seven sections (adds `switches`); MR-42 is the new Mock rule (the switch's device lead, the record's fields). Changes rows added to both specs. `rules.py --base HEAD --renames renames.txt` → 0.
- **Tests, Mock** (`crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/tests/mock_radio.rs`, all green): `mr_16_s8_switch_same_length_starts_on_the_boundary` (x3xx: boundary 2 018 000, old repeat 14 000 samples, seven wraps, new period 2 000), `mr_16_s9_switch_different_length_starts_on_the_boundary` (1 000-sample waveform: same boundary, new period 1 000), `mr_16_s8_record_fields` (the row's exact fields, `applied`), `mr_16_s10_second_pending_switch_refused`, `mr_16_s10_switch_on_stopped_or_finite_or_not_started_refused` (three cases), `mr_16_pending_switch_ends_at_its_boundary`, `mr_25_s11_stop_cancels_pending_switch` (Stop bound 2 016 003 ≤ boundary: `cancelled`, no new-waveform burst), `mr_25_s11_stop_before_the_boundary_applies_then_cuts_the_switch` (bound 2 020 003 > boundary: `applied`, the new repeat handed to 2 020 000, its horizon `now + window`), `mr_25_s11_loss_and_provider_stop_cancel_pending_switch` (loss, `Provider::stop` in both modes; on `ideal`, see D45), `mr_16_metadata_is_empty_or_the_mark_only` (busy stream: empty metadata refused by overlap, others refused, the mark alone accepted; idle stream: empty accepted, other metadata refused), `mr_16_switch_boundary_uses_the_profile_device_lead` (x3xx 2 018 000 against 2 016 000 without the lead; `ideal` 6 000 after a receipt at 5 000). `mr_03_profile_values_reach_the_capabilities_and_the_envelope_section` takes `switches` in its section list (seven sections).

**Deviations (rev 12).**

- **D42, S11 "Stop at the boundary cancels" is not what the Mock does; the implementation follows Q-2.** S11's test `mr_25_s11_stop_at_boundary_cancels_switch` (Stop at `t` equal to the boundary) is not written. The Stop bound is `t + W + 3` (`W` the in-flight window, 10 ms on x3xx), so a Stop at `t = b` has a bound after `b`: the switch is applied, and the new repeat is handed to the horizon `t + W`, as MR-25's held bursts are. Cancellation needs a bound at or before `b` (Q-2). The test that the rule's text supports is `mr_25_s11_stop_before_the_boundary_applies_then_cuts_the_switch`. **For the orchestrator:** S11's two expectations (cancelled at `t = b`, and Q-2) cannot both hold with a nonzero window; this follows Q-2 and MR-25.
- **D43, a 3-sample gap (INFERRED, not reached by a test).** A switch whose boundary lies in `[stop_k − 3, stop_k)` is settled `applied` by `halt` (the record's rule `b < stop`), while the Mock's held cut for that burst may leave the new repeat with no sample. The row would then disagree with what is radiated. Not written as a test; the width is the held-tail width (RM-16). Upgrade with a test once the Mock's cut is pinned.
- **D44, row numbers.** N47S5-61 is taken by 5c's D24 (`ur_21_a_late_joined_burst_cannot_move_and_is_refused`), so the advance rows are N47S5-70 (`prune`) and N47S5-71 (`switch`), both killed by `rm_16_advance_runs_inside_every_call`. Rows 34, 36, 37 and 35 are anchored in `staging.rs` (the record's code), not in `lib.rs`; the killing tests are the Mock tests (34, 36, 37) and the pure one (35). `N47S5-62` is still used twice (5a's and 5c's rows); it is not changed here.
- **D45, the loss and `Provider::stop` tests run on `ideal`, not `x3xx`.** On `x3xx` a `device_lost` fault did not fire in this harness, even at a step at its own instant (`Harness::new("x3xx", …)`, fault at 2 000 000 000 ns and step at the same instant: `Ok`; the same on `ideal`: `DeviceLost`). Not diagnosed. It may be the harness (no clock or stream is started by it) or a Mock fault on `x3xx`; the MR-20 tests use `ideal` for the same reason. **Open:** the orchestrator should decide whether it is a bug (an MR-20 case for `x3xx` is then missing).
- **D46, tests merged.** `mr_16_s8_period_stays_locked_with_same_length` is not written as its own test: its assertions (the switch row's `new_period`, the new burst's wraps) are in `mr_16_s8_switch_same_length_starts_on_the_boundary`, which is row 38's killer. `mr_16_s10_switch_on_stopped_or_finite_or_not_started_refused` covers the three cases in one test (each its own harness), and the lost/provider-stop test covers the loss and both `Provider::stop` modes.
- **D47, F8 is UHD-only.** The F8 recovery does not exist in the Mock, so `mr_25_s11_loss_and_provider_stop_and_f8_cancel_pending_switch` is written without F8 (`mr_25_s11_loss_and_provider_stop_cancel_pending_switch`). F8 stays in §4.4's UHD rows (5d-U).
- **D48, `install` is deferred to 5d-U.** The Mock installs at receipt, so the UHD cursor rule (install before the boundary sample, S8, S12) has no Mock test. §4.4's `ur_21_s8_…` and `ur_21_s12_…` rows are unchanged and belong to 5d-U.
- **D49, `halt` does not call `advance`.** Plan §1.2 said every record call runs `advance` first. `halt` settles the pending switch itself (applied or cancelled by its boundary against `stop`), which gives the same outcome as `advance` followed by the cut, so it needs no `advance` call. The `advance` rows (70, 71) and the pure test cover `prune` and `switch`; the doc comment on `advance` says so.
- **D50, the period and origin of the playing repeat are the Provider's, not the record's.** `Bookings` holds no period (the switch carries `id` and `boundary` only); the Mock holds `TxStream.playing: Option<Playing { id, origin, period }>`, set on every accepted repeat, and reads it for the boundary and the record's `old_period`. UHD (5d-U) needs the same `(origin, period)` from its own state: a stream-level value, not a record field.

## Changes in rev 11 (5c follow-up: D28 and the two survivors, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **D28 done: `core.held` removed.** Production: the field and its initialiser (`core.rs`); the insert at receipt (`control.rs` `book_burst`); the remove and insert of the moved start in `dispatch_start` (`tx.rs`); `Tx::forget` and its 16 `self.forget` statements (`tx.rs`, one of them inside `cancel_unstarted`). `cancel_unstarted` loses its `held` parameter; `txp_give` (the test rig) loses its `core` parameter, its only use being the removed insert (59 callers). `for held in …` loops that no longer read `held` become `for _ in …`. `halt_failed_reopen` and the failed-reopen path keep their report and their take of the open burst. Grep (`crates/`): no `core.held`; the owner's `Tx::held` and `Bookings` are the only representations.
- **Tests.** The `lock(&core.held).insert/extend` setup lines are deleted (they fed no production reader). Reads are replaced by the same fact from the owner's held queue, or deleted where a `tx.held` assertion already stood in the same test: `ur_22_stop_and_loss_during_owner_recovery_release_the_claim` (Stop: `tx.held.is_empty()`; the pending duplicate deleted); `ur_22_underflow_close_failure_reopens_before_the_next_payload` (B and the later burst: `!tx.held.contains_key`); `ur_26_provider_stop_ends_recovery_after_stop_and_keeps_shutdown_report_reads` (shutdown: `tx.held.is_empty()`); `ur_22_underflow_and_loss_end_the_damaged_burst` and `ur_21_first_dispatch_rechecks_all_late_policies` (`!tx.held.contains_key(&20_000)`); `ur_22_a_stop_during_the_configure_leaves_no_payload_after_it` (`tx.held.is_empty()` alone); `ur_22_a_provider_stop_during_the_configure_keeps_the_mark_and_cancels_the_burst` (`tx.held.is_empty()`). Deleted as duplicates of an assertion on `tx.held` in the same test: the lines in `ur_22_nonterminal_reopen_failures_cancel_held_initial_bursts`, `ur_25_an_end_booked_after_the_first_read_cancels_the_held_burst_and_releases_its_reservation`, `ur_26_stop_cancels_the_held_burst_record_only`, `ur_23_a_clock_end_cancels_a_held_burst_and_releases_its_reservation`, `ur_25_a_burst_on_a_clock_that_never_started_is_cancelled_and_releases_its_reservation`, `ur_26_shutdown_after_loss_cancels_held_bursts_record_only`, `ur_23_m4c_a_stop_during_a_continuation_s_wait_ends_the_device_burst_once`, and `ur_22_stop_and_loss…` (pending). The four control-side reads (`control.rs`) become reads of the **record**, `Bookings::stageable` (`TxBusy { until: At(end) }` while a booking holds): the moved start's booking (`ur_21_the_receipt_site_reserves_the_moved_start_before_any_hand_over`), the origin's (`ur_21_a_burst_before_its_clock_s_origin_is_late`), and the held-count pair of `ur_26_d14_a_layout_instance_names_its_streams` (the receive Stop leaves the transmit booking). `ur_21_a_value_after_the_hand_over_and_before_the_end_is_refused`: the `clear()` is deleted (it fed no reader; the refusal is the record's).
- **Rows** (`plan/maintenance/tools/mutations.json`, `plan/phase7/tools/mutations.json`; text edits only; `rules.py --renames renames.txt` → 0). Re-anchored, same mutant program, each probed KILLED by its named killer (`probes-c2/`): P1208 (`if self.open.take().is_some() {` now carries the report; mutant `let _ = self.open.take();`), N47S5-31 (`if moved.is_err() { self.time_error(…)`; mutant `if false { self.time_error(…)`), P1199 and P1206 (`for held in` → `for _ in`; these two were not in the brief: rules.py found them, `old` no longer occurs). Deleted, with evidence: N48S2-01 (its mutant removes a reservation that no longer exists; no same-behaviour mutant), phase7 U17 and U36 (below).
- **U17 (the repeat not ended at the next burst's start): deleted.** It survives the whole fake binary (`probes-c/whole-U17.log`, 338 pass) and is killed in the whole lib binary by `ur_23_m4_a_continuation_waits_without_a_device_call`, `ur_23_m4b_…`, `ur_23_m4c_…` and two more (`probes-c2/U17.log`): those tests start an open repeat at 3 000 and hold a burst at 9 600 inside its span, a state the receipt rule refuses. A repeat's chain runs to the end of time (`Bookings::accept`, `front = i64::MAX`), so a later booking clashes unless it lies before the repeat's start; and a `Stop` reaches the owner as `TxCmd::Stop` on the same ordered channel, before any burst booked after it (`control.rs` Stop arm, `to_tx.send(TxCmd::Stop)`), and `stop_now` ends the open burst. So no reachable state has a held burst at `k > next` while the open burst is a repeat. The defensive cut in `step`'s continuation branch stays; the row is deleted because its only killers test a state outside the receipt rule (the brief's rule for an unreachable behaviour).
- **U36 (`join_open`'s decision instant): deleted.** The mutant `clock.at_or_after(self.core.now()) + 0 * lead_k` for `bound - lead_k` survives the whole lib binary (`probes-c2/U36.log`, 398 pass). Equivalence in every reachable state: `decided_at(now, origin, lead) = max(now, origin - lead)`, so `at_or_after(decided_at)` equals `at_or_after(now)` whenever `now ≥ origin - lead`; and `bound - lead_k` equals `at_or_after(decided_at)` in the join branch (`bound = max(C, next)`, `C = D + lead_k`, `next < C`). A held burst at a join was received at or after `origin - lead` (a receipt before it is late and moved to at least the origin, or refused), and time does not run back, so `now ≥ origin - lead` holds at every join. The only divergence (a joined start before the origin with `now < origin - lead`) needs a state no receipt produces. **No test kills it; the assertion the brief asked for ("a continuation at exactly the open burst's next sample") does not diverge: at `next ≥ origin` both sides judge on time.**
- **Probes** (`probes-c2/`; unmutated baseline PASS first; throwaway copy under `/tmp`, removed): P1208 KILLED (`ur_22_underflow_close_failure_reopens_before_the_next_payload`); N47S5-31 KILLED (`ur_23_s3_dispatch_move_into_a_later_booking_is_refused`); P1199 KILLED (`ur_22_tx_stop_preserves_recovery_for_later_bursts`); P1206 KILLED (`ur_22_nonterminal_reopen_failures_cancel_held_initial_bursts`); U17 and U36 as above. A probe killed by a compile error is not a kill; none did.
