**Deviations (rev 10).**

- **D24, the late joined piece: refused.** Chosen over a record split. `Bookings::move_start` (`staging.rs`) already returns `Overlap` for a joined piece (`c.start < old && old <= c.last`), so `dispatch_start` takes its existing refusal path. Extending the record to split a piece changes the chain's region and end in a way that is not small. Shortcut: a late joined piece is refused where RM-14 would otherwise move it. Upgrade when the record can split a piece. Test: `ur_21_a_late_joined_burst_cannot_move_and_is_refused` (manual clock; C joins A at its next sample, late; C refused, A goes on); row N47S5-61. A refused piece keeps its region in the record, since `drop_piece` does nothing to a piece (rev 9, D25), so a later booking there is refused until the chain is handed over (INFERRED; no test drives it).
- **D28, `core.held` NOT removed.** Count (grep, `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src`): production code has no reader left. Its uses are writes: `control.rs:518` (insert), `tx.rs:351` (`forget`: remove), `tx.rs:1004–1007` (`dispatch_start`: remove then insert). Its reads are all in tests: 72 lines in `tx.rs` (from line 1252) and 6 in `control.rs`. Removal needs: the field and its init (`core.rs:772, 846`), `forget` and its 16 call sites in `src`, the three writes, rows that anchor on `self.forget` (P1208, N48S2-01, N47S5-31), and the 78 test lines, some of whose tests exist only to check the set and would be rewritten or deleted with a replacement named. Not done: it is a multi-file change with its own rows, and the step's context was used up. Left for the next sub-step of 5c or a follow-up; the state above is what it starts from.
- **D36, `preemption_tests` renamed, not deleted.** Its test `mr_17_handed_front_reduction_cases` tests the payload helpers that production uses (`handed_end`, `merge_front`, `handed_end_before`), so the module stays as `payload_cut_tests`.

Two fake tests that used its group now use `measure_min_timed_command_lead` (`radio.timing.min_timed_command_lead_ns`): `a_companion_that_fails_after_a_solo_measurement_keeps_the_solo_value` and `provider_measurement_groups_fake_rehearsal_round_trips_without_claiming_measurements`.

## Changes in rev 9 (5b-3, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **5b-3 done**: uhd-tx's dispatch (`dispatch_start`, `tx.rs`) takes a SendAsap move of a head through the record: `Bookings::move_start(old, new, len)` (`staging.rs`) tests the moved region against every other chain, the Stop region and the head's joined pieces (M4), and relocates on success; a refusal calls `Bookings::drop_piece(start, len)`, the head's region leaves the overlap rule and its end stays in the value rule (C6). The refusal is `TIME_ERROR { outcome: refused }` and `COMMAND_REJECTED` carrying the Action's id (`held.settings.action()`). The configure refusal (`configure_turn`'s error arm) also drops the region (C6, N47S4-13b).
- **Spec**: RM-14 (`design/07`, one sentence: a dispatch move is judged as at receipt, joined pieces included; a joined piece made a head by host lateness is not moved in the record); UR-21 (`design/18`, one paragraph on the dispatch move, and its test line names the two 5b-3 tests). `rules.py` passes with `--renames renames.txt`.
- **Tests**: new `ur_23_s3_dispatch_move_into_a_later_booking_is_refused` (three blocks: Sol 3's sequence; the M4 sequence with B joined at `s+40`; a free move sent at the moved start) and `ur_23_s3_refused_move_frees_its_front`. Rewritten by subject: `ur_23_txs_late_preemption_uses_the_handed_frontier` (§6.1, REWRITE as `ur_23_s3_…`; see D31). Rewritten: `ur_21_first_dispatch_refuses_a_moved_start_collision` (`tx.rs`, bookings through `txp_book`: the start set no longer decides; D33). Renamed (D32): `ur_23_a_burst_booked_at_a_closed_short_burst_s_end_is_late` → `ur_23_a_burst_at_a_closed_short_burst_s_end_is_late_at_dispatch`. Added (D31): `ur_23_a_burst_at_the_handed_frontier_continues_the_open_burst`.
- **Rows** (`plan/maintenance/tools/mutations.json`): N47S5-31 (the dispatch's collision check), N47S5-32 (`drop_piece` keeps the region), N47S5-59 (M4: the head's joined pieces skipped) added; N47S4-13b re-anchored to the configure refusal's drop (D35); N48S3-10 and P1166 retargeted to their killers' new names (D31, D34). Probes (throwaway copies, `probes-b3/`): N47S5-31, -59 KILLED on the M4 and Sol 3 `send_asap` assertions (`tx.rs`), N47S5-32 KILLED on the record's `Overlap` in the free-space booking, N47S4-13b KILLED on the value refusal, N48S3-10 KILLED on its renamed killer, P1166 KILLED on the owner's in-window assertion. Each baseline PASS.

**Deviations (rev 9).**

- **D24, joined piece made a head.** A joined piece dispatched as a head (host lateness, m8) has no chain whose `start` is its key, so `move_start` returns `Ok` without touching the record and the Held moves unchecked. RM-14 says so. The one shortcut: a late piece's move is not judged against later bookings. Upgrade when the record can split a piece (before 5c deletes `preempt`, whose `h == next` SendAsap has the same gap, see §10 below).
- **D25, pieces keep their span.** `drop_piece` and a successful move both remove only the head's region: a chain with joined pieces keeps those pieces from the first piece's start (`remove_head`, `Chain.start = old + len`). A pieced head's move succeeds only when `new ≥ chain.front` (its region starts at `old + len + 2 > old + len`, so any earlier `new` meets the first piece), so the moved head never overlaps the pieces it leaves.
- **D26, `drop_piece` takes the head's length.** §1.2 lists `drop_piece(start)`; the pieces' span starts at `start + len`, so the call is `drop_piece(start, len)`.
- **D27, the configure refusal drops the region.** `configure_turn`'s error arm (`tx.rs`) calls `drop_piece` before `device_failed`: the burst that the configure refused leaves the overlap rule and keeps its end (C6). The doc comment of `configure_turn` already said the burst "stays counted in its `Bookings` to its booked end". N47S4-13b's mutant is now the reset of that same line (`busy = Default::default()`, end included).
- **D28, `core.held` kept.** `dispatch_start` still updates the start set (`remove` then `insert` at the moved start); `preempt` and `book_burst` still read or write it until 5c. §1.4 names it as removable once its readers are only overlap tests; after 5c it has no overlap reader and can go (5c's check by grep).
- **D29, C6 is not isolated by the Sol 3 sequence.** The refusing booking starts at or after the moved front, which is past the refused head's booked end, so its own end always dominates `until`. The value part of C6 for a dispatch refusal rests on `ur_21_a_refused_burst_stays_counted_until_its_booked_end` (the configure path, N47S4-13b). No new value assertion was written, since it could not discriminate.
- **D30, the freed span.** §4.2 says "a booking inside the moved span is accepted". The moved span holds Sol 3's later booking, so a booking there is refused. `ur_23_s3_refused_move_frees_its_front` books at `30 000` (inside the refused head's own span `[20 000, 40 002)`): the mutant that keeps the front (N47S5-32) refuses it.
- **D31, the rewritten preemption test.** `ur_23_txs_late_preemption_uses_the_handed_frontier` had three cases on a preempted burst. Its move case is now the Sol 3 block 3 (free space: sent at the moved start, one `send_asap`); its refusal is blocks 1–2; the drop case is not kept (a booking inside the open repeat's span is refused at receipt, and dispatch's drop is covered by `ur_23_a_first_send_one_tick_inside_its_lead_follows_drop_and_flag`). Its on-time frontier case survives as `ur_23_a_burst_at_the_handed_frontier_continues_the_open_burst` (the continuation at the handed frontier, no end-of-burst between; the killer of P1166, D34).
- **D32, D14: `ur_23_a_burst_booked_at_a_closed_short_burst_s_end_is_late`.** It gives both bursts with `txp_give` (no record), so every assertion is a dispatch verdict (the handed front, `late_policy_front_outcome`). Nothing was dropped. Renamed `…_at_dispatch`, with a comment that the booking path is pinned by `s3_parity_booking_after_a_closed_short_burst_follows_d5`. `renames.txt` records the rename; N48S3-10's filter and `design/18`'s test lines follow it.
- **D33.** `ur_21_first_dispatch_refuses_a_moved_start_collision` books through `txp_book`: with the start set no longer the collision check, the test's bursts must be in the record (as `control.rs` books them).
- **D34, P1166.** Its killer was the rewritten test (D31). P1166 (`if h <= next` → `if h < next`) is killed by the continuation test instead; the row is still deleted in 5c with preemption (§6.2).
- **D35, row set.** N47S5-31, -32 and -59 are new (§5's 5b-3 rows; the anchors are token anchors for `tx.rs`, and exact `staging.rs` tokens for -32 and -59, D15's crate pattern: the mutated `ezsdr-radio` is built under the UHD killer). N47S5-32 mutates `staging.rs` (`drop_piece`'s `remove_head`).

**Preemption paths this step makes unreachable (for 5c; INFERRED, no test drives them).**

1. `step`'s `Some(&k) if k < c => preempt` (`tx.rs` ~452–455): a held head that starts before the device chain's next start. A booking that overlaps an open span is refused at receipt (`accept`), and a dispatch move that meets one is refused (`move_start`), so no held head can start before the open burst's span. Delete with 5c as §3 already says.
2. `step`'s `h < next` branch of `if h <= next { preempt }` (`tx.rs` ~433–436): the same reasoning for a held head before the open burst's next sample. The `h == next` case stays (M3: the switch's install and the joined continuation).
3. `preempt`'s identical-start refusal (`tx.rs` ~1043–1049) is reachable only from 1 and 2, but **not** only through them: `preempt` with `h == next` and a late decision (host lateness on a joined piece, D24) still moves the piece with the start set and no record check. This path is **still reachable** after 5b-3. 5c deletes `preempt` and its SendAsap branch with it; if 5c is split, this move must go through `move_start` first.

**Still open for 5c (not changed here).** D8 (the UR-21 preemption paragraph and the UR-23 wording, the two preemption tests `ur_23_a_preemption_leaving_n_closes_the_device_burst` and `ur_23_m7_…` pass unchanged under 5b-3), §6.2's preemption rows, and §8's 5c list.

## Changes in rev 8 (5b-2b, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **5b-2b done**: the owner's close on the record (UR-23, RM-16). `Bookings::continuation_open(e, now, join_by)` and `Bookings::close(e)` (`staging.rs`; `Chain.last` added, read by `continuation_open`); `Core::join_by` (`core.rs`, `J(e)` shared with `accept` through `control.rs`'s `book_burst`); the owner's `close_unjoined` (`tx.rs`) takes the streams lock, reads `now` under it, and closes only when `continuation_open` is false; the Q-6 stat `tx_close_past_record_front`.
- **Spec**: UR-23 (the held sample's close at `J(e)`, with its `*Test (held-tail close):*` line) in `design/18-uhd-radio.md`; RM-16 (the owner's close, its `*Test:*` names) in `design/07-radio-model.md`. UR-21 (preemption) is unchanged (5c).
- **Tests**: `ur_21_s4b_three_bookings_b_continues_a_and_c_plays_on_time`, `ur_21_m1_148us_booked_early_at_the_chain_front_plays_on_time`, `ur_21_m1_held_tail_waits_for_a_join_received_before_its_txcmd_is_drained`, `ur_21_m1_receipt_at_the_deadline_is_decided_by_the_record`, `ur_21_stat_counts_owner_closes_past_the_record_front` (UHD `tx.rs`, manual clock), and `s15_parity_s4b_three_bookings_b_continues_a_and_c_plays_on_time` (`parity.rs`).
- **Rows**: N47S5-25, -56, -57, -58, -60 added to `mutations.json`; each probed in a throwaway copy (`probes-b2b/summary-final.txt`): all five KILLED by their named killer, each baseline PASS. `phase7/B09` retargeted (D19, KILLED). The parity test FAILS on the pre-change code (5b-2a's `tx.rs`, `staging.rs`, `core.rs`, `control.rs`), so it does catch the immediate close.

- **Deviation D15, files of rows 57 and 58.** Both mutate `crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs` (`continuation_open`'s `last == e` case; `accept`'s `now <= join_by(start)`), not `tx.rs` as §5 says, where the record's functions live. Their `crate` is `ezsdr-radio-uhd` (`--lib`), so the mutated `ezsdr-radio` is built under the UHD killer.
- **Deviation D16, row 25 re-anchored.** `Some(_) => end_open(true)` no longer exists (the owner calls `close_unjoined`). The row is now `if busy.continuation_open(…) {` → `if false {`, the immediate close restored. Its killer fails at the test's own booking helper: once A closes at C's booking, B is refused at receipt (`Overlap`), not at a send assertion. The pre-change parity test fails the same way on UHD.
- **Deviation D17, row 56.** The anchor is the owner's closure `|k| self.core.join_by(clock, k)` (one match in `tx.rs`); the mutant `|k| clock.instant(k) - self.core.ticks(DEVICE_LEAD_NS)` is the lead deadline the plan names.
- **Deviation D18, row 60 and the stat.** The stat counts only closes of a join chain: `Bookings::close` returns a front only for `join && end == e`. A standalone head's close has no record front to pass, so it is not counted. Row 60 is `let _ = 1;` in place of the increment.
- **Deviation D19, `phase7/B09` retargeted.** Its old text was the lead-deadline close (`None if clock.instant(c) - now <= DEVICE_LEAD_NS`), removed by §3. The row now replaces `Some(_) | None => return self.close_unjoined(clock, c),` with `Some(_) => return …, None => {}`: a burst with nothing booked behind it is never closed. Killer unchanged (`ur_23_a_burst_alone_is_ended_before_it_runs_dry`, `fake`): KILLED on underflow.
- **Deviation D20, five UHD tests moved to the record.** At the first step after `J(e)` the close comes 5 ms before the end, not 2 ms, so bursts that were only `txp_give`n closed too early against their own booking. They are now booked through `txp_book` (the record takes the booking, as `control.rs` does; `txp_record` is its record half, added so a test can book before the hand-over): `ur_23_txs_one_shots_keep_their_payload_and_end_on_the_last_sample` (start 500 000 → 10 000 000, first `device_at` at `start + len − 1 200 000`), `ur_23_a_33_sample_burst_sends_32_then_holds_one` (k 3 000 → 6 000, expectations moved with it), `ur_23_a_continuation_of_two_samples_is_sent_at_once_with_no_minimum`, `ur_23_m4c_…` (second part) and `ur_23_m9_a_waiting_continuation_leaves_the_held_tail_alone`. No expectation of a sample value or count changed.
- **Deviation D21, the parity list is not in the repo.** §4.6 names `s15_parity_booking_rule_both_receipt_orders` (S1–S5 and S4c, 5b-2a). No such test exists in `parity.rs`: 5b-2a did not write it. This step adds only `s15_parity_s4b_three_bookings_b_continues_a_and_c_plays_on_time` (standalone, both Providers, both sides' bursts and sends). Writing the 5b-2a list is open for the orchestrator.
- **Deviation D22, the preemption tests kept.** `ur_23_a_preemption_leaving_n_closes_the_device_burst` and `ur_23_m7_a_preempting_burst_is_not_held_up_by_a_wait` (§6.1 says DELETE in 5b-2) pass under the new close, and their premise is preemption (5c's). They stay until 5c.
- **Deviation D23, the stat on the parity runs.** The parity tests live in another crate and cannot read the UHD stat. The zero is asserted in the UHD S4b test (`past_front(&core) == 0`), and the parity test checks the bursts and sends.
- **No idle-sleep cap (R4-m2)** as the ruling says: the owner closes at the first step after `J(e)`. A booking received after `J(e)` is refused at receipt (§1.3), so the step's latency cannot admit one.

## Changes in rev 7 (5b-2a, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **5b-2a done**: the receipt rule (RM-15) on both Providers, `first_free` for SendAsap (RM-14), the Stop region (RM-16, `halt`), pruning, `ordinary_k`, `stop_sample`, `stop_bound`. Rows N47S5-18 to -24, -27 to -30 and N47S5-68 are probed in a throwaway copy (`probes-b2a/summary-final.txt`): all 18 rows KILLED by their named killer. The first pass had one SURVIVED (N47S5-28: its test booked the dropped burst at sample 0, whose span missed the value it checked); the test was rewritten (a dropped burst at 11 999 and an on-time one at 12 002) and re-probed KILLED.
- **Spec**: RM-13, RM-14, RM-15, RM-16 (`design/07`); MR-16, MR-17, MR-25, MR-32 (`design/09`); UR-21, UR-22 (`design/18`); KL-36 (symptom "refused at receipt"; padded-end numbers and Ceiling kept).
- **Deviation D5, pruning bound.** `Bookings::prune` is called with `now_k = clock.at_or_after(receipt)`, not with `ordinary_k` (Q-5). `now_k` is the earlier, conservative bound: no chain a booking received now could meet is dropped. The cost is a few chains kept until a later booking prunes them.
- **Deviation D6, Mock front machinery.** `tx_handed_front` returns the payload end only; `remember_tx_front`, `TxStream.front`, `short_burst_floor`, `DEVICE_MIN_FIRST_SEND` and the `late_policy_front_outcome` call are removed from the Mock. `TxFront` stays for `stop_cut`. The Mock's `late_by` is now the policy's own value.
- **Deviation D7, refusals.** The Mock's `held.contains_key` and `open.next` refusals are removed (covered by `accept`). The UHD `core.held` insert stays unconditional, since `tx.rs` dispatch reads it until 5b-3. Refusal reasons: `MR-16: burst overlaps an accepted booking` (Mock), `UR-21: the burst overlaps an accepted booking` (UHD); the old "a held burst already has this start" is removed. `Bookings::book(end)` is called only when `accept` succeeds.
- **Deviation D8, preemption still live.** The UR-21 preemption paragraph and the UR-23 device-burst wording still describe uhd-tx's preemption: `Tx::preempt` (`tx.rs:1020`) and its calls (`tx.rs:435, 453`) remain in the code, and the preemption tests (`ur_23_a_preemption_leaving_n_closes_the_device_burst`, `ur_23_m7_…`) still pass. They go in 5c and 5b-2b as §5c and §6.1 say. The spec's UR-21 sentence is changed in this step (refused at receipt).
- **Deviation D9, parity scope (S3 Stop variants).** `s3_parity_booking_after_a_closed_short_burst_follows_d5` and `s3_parity_booking_after_a_device_burst_s_short_tail_follows_d5` assert exact expectations only when the Stop is at 0. For Stops at 1–2 samples they assert only that the Mock and UHD agree: a Stop before B places B in the Stop tail (S7), a different sequence.
- **Deviation D10, S3(b) and S5.** S3(b) is restricted to long-first; the short-first order is the S5 case, and `s5_parity_b_received_before_a_refuses_a` covers "B received before A".
- **Deviation D11, S13 parity.** `s13_parity_b_refused_at_receipt_and_the_value_answers` books B early (at 91 ms), because the UHD rig's clock runs ahead of submitted ticks by up to about 1.2 ms. The value is placed at exact `s + 5` samples through `tx_root_after`.
- **Deviation D12, refusal count.** `overlap_refusals` counts refusal rows, since a rejected row carries no booking target and its `at` is the receipt time.
- **Deviation D13, sized lengths.** The prepared lengths of the `sized` cases are `[4, 10, 31, 32, 40, 100]`.
- **Killer substitutions (named in §5, not existing)**: row 23 → `mr_25_stop_does_not_radiate_unhanded_window_edge_payload`; rows 24 and 30 → `ur_23_stop_preserves_handed_cursor_after_stop`; row 28 → `ur_21_a_burst_dropped_at_booking_is_not_booked`; row 29 → `mr_25_s7_stop_tail_refuses_until_bound`. S4c has no separate test: `s3_parity_booking_after_a_device_burst_s_short_tail_follows_d5` covers it on both Providers.
- **Re-anchored rows**: N47S5-12 and -68 (the `joins` signature); N47S4-17c and -43 (the single `stop_at` site in `core.rs`, which both entry points share); N47S4-08b (the `book` call's moved end); N47S4-45 (`busy.book(None)` on the same call); J315 (`Some(free) => start = free` → `Some(_) => {}`). Same program, token-identical.

## Changes in rev 6 (5b-1, implementer 2026-11-07)

- **Deviation D1, values stay in root ticks.** §1.1 and §0 move the value rule to samples (`value_k`). The two forms are equal by the §0 algebra (`instant(e) ≤ t ⟺ e ≤ value_k(t)`), so 5b-1 keeps `Bookings::until` and `stageable` in root ticks and moves only the behaviour that changes: the Stop bound. Moving the value rule would thread the stream's clock through `Staging::set` (seven arguments, every test call site) for no behaviour. The record's chains stay in samples (5b-2). `value_k` is not written; row **N47S5-15 is not used** (its mutant has no target), and its killer `rm_21_value_at_the_exact_end_instant_is_accepted` is not written: `rm_21_a_transmit_value_at_the_exclusive_end_is_accepted_and_one_sample_before_refused` pins the exact end in root ticks.
- **Deviation D2, the Stop bound.** `stop_k(t, W) = at_or_after(t + W) + 3` is written as `staging::stop_bound(at, in_flight, at_or_after, instant)`, which returns the root tick `instant(stop_k)`; `Bookings::stop(bound)` takes it. Each Provider passes its stream clock (Mock `time::k_at_or_after` and `time::v_of`; UHD `Clock::at_or_after` and `Clock::instant`). Mock's `None` on overflow maps to `i64::MAX` (the stream never ends a repeat), which is unreachable for real sample counts.
- **Deviation D3, S13 parity not written.** `s13_parity_value_after_busy_case` needs a receipt on an exact sample instant on both Providers. The S14 comment in `parity.rs` (the UHD rig's device clock runs ahead of each submitted tick by up to about 1.2 ms) shows the parity rig cannot pin one. The exact boundary is pinned per Provider (§4.3's changed tests, which now assert `bound − 1` refused and `bound` taken). N47S5-01's killer is therefore its 5a pure test (`rm_21_busy_is_max_accepted_end`).
- **Deviation D4, mutation rows 16 and 17 have one filter each.** Row 16's killers are four (the pure test, the Mock test and two UHD tests); the row schema allows one filter, so each killer is probed and logged separately (`probes-b1b/summary.txt`). Row 17's killer is the pure fractional-window test.
- **Rows re-anchored (§6.2)**: N47S4-17 (`at_or_after(at)`, the window dropped), N47S4-17c and N47S4-43 (the UHD bound text), N47S4-18c (the Mock bound text). Each still has its killer in §4.3. N47S4-17b keeps its text (`end.min(bound)`).
- **Spec**: `design/07-radio-model.md` RM-16 (the Stop bound `B`) and RM-21 (`min(busy end, B)`); `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md` KL-35 (the value wait is the window plus three samples).

### E. Corrections found while writing rev 3 (not rulings)

- **E1. The value rule is one number, not per-end caps.** Rev 2 §1.1 `stop: Option<i64>` capped every end, including the end of a booking made after a Stop. Today's `Busy` (`staging.rs:70-107`) caps only the ends present at the Stop and lets a later booking extend the wait (`Busy::book` after `Busy::stop`). Rev 3 keeps today's single `until` (m2): a Stop caps it, a later booking extends it. Behaviour-neutral in 5a; a later booking's end is never capped by an earlier Stop.
- **E2. `book` keeps its name for the value part.** Rows N47S4-18b and -45 name `busy.book(end)`. The receipt rule is therefore `accept(start, len, now, join_by)` and the Stop region is `halt(t, stop_k)` (§1.2). Same for `first_free`, `repeat_boundary` (§1.2).
- **E3. A repeat's open front becomes `stop_k` at a Stop** (M2 cannot mean "never lower a repeat's MAX front": the repeat must end at the Stop). Finite fronts are never lowered (M2).
- **E4. Rev 2's row 09 and 10 pure tests were wrong as written.** `book(s,45)` after `book(s+50,40)` does not run into the later booking (`s+47 < s+50`); the discriminating case is `book(s,55)` refused and `book(s,10)` accepted (§4.1 row 07). Corrected.
- **E5. `ordinary_k`, `value_k` and `stop_k` are not in 5a's pure rule** (5a's value rule stays in root ticks, as rev 2 says).
- **E6. `Busy` is `Clone`, not `Copy`** once it holds chains. The closures that return it copy it with `.clone()` (the two rows that name `Busy::default()` are re-anchored, §6.2).

### B. Corrections found while re-reading the code (not rulings)

- **C1. The overlap rule is two-sided.** Rev 1's table (§1.2) only refused starts inside `[s, a)`. A candidate that starts before an accepted booking and runs into it must also be refused (its own front or payload reaches the booking). Rev 1 accepted it. §1.3 states the two-sided rule.
- **C2. Rev 1's `h <= next` branch is not the switch refusal.** `tx.rs:433-438` is dormant once receipt refuses overlaps and is deleted in 5c. The switch's refusal is the cursor rule (§4.4).
- **C3. The `N47S4-03…-53` range is not a preemption family.** They are stage 4 value rows (§6.3). None is deleted.
- **C4. Python line.** The test that rev 1 called "~185-215" is `test_ea_16_repeat_uses_stream_membership_without_setting_it` at `python/tests/test_easy_api.py:728`; `test_ea_16_aligned_gives_its_calls_one_instant` is at `:597`.
- **C5. Booking-time SendAsap bounds differ.** UHD moves to `at_or_after(instant(now_k) + lead)` with no front (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/control.rs:472-474`, VERIFIED). Mock moves to `later_bound(ordinary, front)` (`crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/lib.rs:1644`, VERIFIED). One bound on both (§0 `ordinary_k`, §1.2 `first_free`).
- **C6. Cancelled and refused bookings still count for values.** Today `Busy` never removes a booking (`staging.rs:68-69`) and a UHD dispatch refusal leaves it counted (`N47S4-13b`, `tx.rs`). Rev 1 §3.4 said "a cancelled burst still counts" is the wrong answer; it is today's stage 4 answer and stays. Cancellation and refusal remove a booking from the overlap rule only (§1.1 `dropped_ends`).
- **C7. A second copy of the front on UHD.** `TxChain.front` (`tx.rs:114`, `:141`) and the record's fronts are two representations of the same fact. The ruling keeps `chain.front` for the configure guard (`tx.rs:514`) and host lateness (`tx.rs:972-976`); §10 names the risk and §8 5b-2 removes the start set `core.held` (control.rs:475, 494; tx.rs:981-990) if its readers are only overlap tests.

### C. Row renumbering (rev 1 → rev 2)

Rev 1 rows 01–04 keep their numbers. Rev 1 05 (continuation at the exact end) is now 05 with a new meaning (the join). Rev 1 06 (`+3`) is 16. Rev 1 07 (`<` to `<=`) is 08; the two-sided rule is the new 07. Rev 1 09 (UHD identical-start) is 19. Rev 1 10 (Stop cancel) is 10 (pure) and 23–24 (Providers). Rev 1 11 (Drop) is 27–28. Rev 1 12 (SendAsap) is 20–22. Rev 1 13 (Stop tail) is 14 (pure) and 29–30 (Providers). Rev 1 14 (second Stop) is 09. Rev 1 15 (repeat boundary) is 13 and 33. Rev 1 16 (pending check) is 34. Rev 1 17 (Stop cancels pending) is 36. Rev 1 18 (cursor, reverse) is removed and replaced by 42. Rev 1 19 (install before boundary) is 46. Rev 1 20 (period lock) is 38. Rev 1 21 (Kernel admission) is removed (Q6) and replaced by 39 and 45. Rev 1 22 (Python second repeat) is 48.

## 0. Coordinates and conversions (Sol 7)

- The record's coordinates are the transmit stream's sample indices `k` on its one clock: `Clock { origin, n }` with `instant(k) = origin + k·n` in root ticks and `at_or_after(t)` the ceiling sample (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/core.rs:232-241`, VERIFIED). One clock per transmit stream per Run (N47S4-35 and N47S4-40 pin it).
- Four definitions, each written once in `ezsdr_radio::staging`, which takes the clock's `at_or_after` as an argument so both Providers pass their own (UHD `core.rs:239`; Mock `time::k_at_or_after`, `lib.rs:1642`):
  - `booking_k(t) = at_or_after(t)`: booking instants, the window and the device lead (ceiling).
  - `value_k(t) = at_or_after(t + 1) − 1`: a value's receipt (floor). For any sample `e`, `instant(e) ≤ t ⟺ e ≤ value_k(t)`, so stage 4's root comparison (`staging.rs:103`) is the same test in samples (VERIFIED by the algebra of `instant` and `at_or_after`).
  - **[m1] `ordinary_k(now, origin, lead) = at_or_after(instant(at_or_after(decided_at(now, origin, lead)) + lead))`**: the first sample a booking received at `now` may start at without being late, written as `decide`'s threshold (`burst.rs:110-111`, `control.rs:452-453,473`, VERIFIED). Mock's `lib.rs:1642` (`at_or_after(decided + lead)`) differs by one sample when `lead/n` is not an integer; the Mock takes this form in 5b-2a. `decided_at` is `timeline.rs:334` (VERIFIED).
  - `stop_k(t, W) = at_or_after(t + W) + 3`: the first sample a booking may start at after a Stop taking effect at root tick `t`. `W` is the window of the profile (`tx_in_flight_ns`, 10 ms in `x3xx.rs:386`, VERIFIED; `ideal` 0, `channels.rs:352`, VERIFIED). The `+3` after a no-tail close is INFERRED (§11). **[m6]** `t` is the control's `now` (`control.rs:311-315`, VERIFIED); the owner may hand one more step before it drains `TxCmd::Stop`, so the Stop's front can pass `stop_k` by one step's duration (host timing: RM-16 and UR-23). Parity tests at `stop_k` use the manual clock.
  - **[M1] `J(e) = instant(e) − (lead + allowance)`**, the join deadline in root ticks: a booking may join a chain at `e` only if its receipt is at or before `J(e)` (§1.3). `lead + allowance` is 5 ms on both Providers (UHD `DEVICE_LEAD_NS + DELIVERY_ALLOWANCE_NS`, `profile.rs:11-21`, VERIFIED; the Mock's x3xx `2 ms + 3 ms`, `crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/profile.rs:47,143-146`, VERIFIED). It is passed in as a function of `e`.
- `repeat_k(origin, N, receipt, W, lead) = first origin + j·N ≥ at_or_after(receipt + W + lead)`, `j ≥ 0`. The lead is passed in (Q5, Q-7): UHD `DEVICE_LEAD_NS` = 2 ms (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/profile.rs:11`, VERIFIED); Mock reads `profile.timing().min_timed_command_lead_ns` (2 ms in the x3xx Mock profile, `crates/ezsdr-mock-radio/src/profile.rs:47`, VERIFIED; 0 in `ideal`, INFERRED from the rulings).
- Grid for the tests: UHD's 200 MHz root at 1 MS/s is 200 root ticks per sample (`n = 200`, `origin ≠ 0`, one fractional root instant per test).

---

## 1. The booking record (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs`)

### 1.1 Data (per transmit stream; replaces `Busy`, `staging.rs:70-107`)

```
Bookings {
  until:  Option<Until>,       // [m2, E1] the one value number: the latest booked end, root ticks; a Stop caps it (as today's `Busy`)
  chains: Vec<Chain>,          // [E2] accepted bookings that occupy time, sample indices (the overlap rule)
  region: Option<(i64, i64)>,  // [M2] the Stop tail: one refused region [start, front) in samples; None before a Stop that cuts anything
  pending: Option<Switch>,     // 5d (§1.2)
}
Chain {
  start: i64,                  // the head's first sample
  last:  i64,                  // [M1] the last piece's first sample; a piece joined at `e` has last = e. Added in 5b-2b with `continuation_open` (5a keeps no field nothing reads)
  end:   i64,                  // the last piece's exclusive end; i64::MAX while a repeat plays
  front: i64,                  // exclusive: no other booking may start in [start, front) (§1.3)
  join:  bool,                 // a booking at exactly `end` continues the chain (until a Stop)
}
```

- **Head longer than 32 samples** (the open first send that is held, 48 S3): `join = true`, `front = start + len + 2`. The `+2` is the held-tail close (S3 bench, VERIFIED on both boards: `hw_b8_burst_one_sample_after_a_burst`, 2026-11-06).
- **Standalone head of at most 32 samples** (one closed device burst, D1): `join = false`, `front = start + max(len, 4) + 1`. That is `start + 5` for 1–3 samples (the padded first send, UR-22 `design/18-uhd-radio.md:250`; `tx.rs:634,749`) and `end + 1` for 4–32.
- **Joined piece** (contiguous with a chain whose `join` is true, §1.3 step 2): the chain's `end` becomes the piece's end and `front = end + 2`, whatever the piece's length (Sol 2). A short piece that joins a long chain takes the chain's front, not its own `end + 1`.
- **Repeat**: `end = MAX`, `front = MAX`, `join = false`. A repeat has no front until a Stop (E3).
- **Stop** (§1.2 `halt`): every kept chain's `front` is capped at `stop_k` (an open repeat becomes finite there, E3); every chain gets `join = false`. **[R5-m1]** `end` is not capped: it is read only with `join`, which the Stop clears. **[R4-1, R4-2, R5-A]** A finite front is lowered to the bound (M2 as first written is superseded); `region` is added only when the Stop cancels a booking that starts before `stop_k`, hulled with an earlier region (§1.2).
- **Value rule (stage 4, RM-21)**: `stageable(r)` holds iff `until` is `At(end)` with `end ≤ r`. `until` is the maximum over every booking's payload end, cancelled and refused bursts included (C6), capped at each Stop as today's `Busy::stop` caps it (E1). In 5a this is root ticks and unchanged. 5b-1 moves it to samples (`value_k`, §0).
- **Payload ends and fronts are kept apart** (Sol 8): a value is judged by `until` alone; a booking by the chains and the region. Nothing stage 4 counted stops counting (C6). **[m2]** Only the maximum end is read, so no list of ends is kept.
- **[Q-5] Pruning** (5b-2a): a chain whose `front ≤ ordinary_k(now)` can no longer block any booking and is dropped; `book` stays O(live chains), not O(bookings) (AGENTS §7 and `lib.rs` booking loop).

### 1.2 Functions (pure in `ezsdr_radio::staging`; each called by both Providers and nothing else)

| Function | Does | Called by (sub-step that wires it) |
|---|---|---|
| `accept(start, len: Option<i64>, now, join_by) -> Result<(), Overlap>` **[E2]** | the receipt rule (§1.3): refuses, or joins the chain whose `join` is true, whose `end` is `start` and whose join deadline holds (`now ≤ join_by(end)`, **[M1]**), or opens a chain. `join_by(e) = J(e)` (§0) | Mock `handle_tx_burst` (replaces `lib.rs:1677-1685`, 5b-2a); UHD `book_burst` (replaces the identical-start insert, `control.rs:494`, 5b-2a); both for a SendAsap-moved start |
| `first_free(k, now, join_by) -> i64` **[R4-m3]** | the smallest sample ≥ `k` outside every chain's `[start, front)` and the region, except a join point (`k = end` of a chain with `join`) that a booking received at `now` may still join (`now ≤ join_by(k)`): the booking-time SendAsap bound | Mock `lib.rs:1644` (5b-2a); UHD `control.rs:472-474` (5b-2a) |
| `move_start(old, new, len) -> Result<(), Overlap>` **[R4-m1]** | dispatch-time move of a chain head: removes `old`; tests the moved head's region `[new, front(new, len))` against every other piece, **and every piece of the head's chain except the head itself** (its joined pieces included) **[M4]**; relocates on success. The head's length is passed in: `Chain` keeps no head end | UHD `dispatch_start`, replaces `tx.rs:982-990` (5b-3) |
| `close(e)` **[R4-4]** | the owner's close at `e` (§3): clears `join` of the chain that ends at `e`, so a join received later is refused by step 4 whatever `now` it carries. Pure; called under the streams lock in the same critical section as `continuation_open` | UHD owner `tx.rs:457-466` (5b-2b) |
| `drop_piece(start)` | the head's region leaves the overlap rule; its end stays in `until` (C6) | UHD refused move (5b-3) |
| `halt(t, stop_k)` **[M2, E2; R4-1, R4-2; R5-A, R5-m1, R5-m2]** | a Stop taking effect at `t` (`t = at_or_after(instant)`). `dropped` is whether a chain starts at or after `t` and before `stop_k` (**[R5-A]** a booking starting at or after `stop_k` was never handed, so its cancel adds no region). The chains starting before `t` are kept with `front` capped at `stop_k` (**[R5-m1]** `end` is not capped); every chain's `join` is cleared (**[R5-m2]** no early return: when nothing is cut, nothing is dropped and the caps change nothing); the chains starting at or after `t` are cancelled. If `dropped`, `region` becomes `[t, stop_k)`, or its hull with an earlier region; otherwise `region` is unchanged | both (Mock `stop_tx`, `lib.rs:1154-1201`; UHD Stop, `control.rs:315`), 5a pure; wired 5b-2a |
| `continuation_open(e, now) -> bool` **[M1; R4-4]** | a chain's last piece was joined at `e` (`last == e` with the chain joined), so the owner waits for that piece's `Held` and never closes at `e`; or a chain with `join` ends at `e` and `now ≤ J(e)`. The join moves `end`, so the first case is what keeps it true after B joins (the re-check's (b)). Read with `now` under the streams lock, in the same section as `close(e)` | UHD owner `tx.rs:457-466` (5b-2b) |
| `stageable(r)` | §1.1 value rule | `Staging::set` (`staging.rs:147-149`) on both (5a in root ticks; 5b-1 in samples) |
| `repeat_boundary(origin, N, bound)` **[E2]** | the first `origin + j·N ≥ bound`, `j ≥ 0`; `bound = at_or_after(receipt + W + lead)` (§0) | `switch` (5d) |
| `switch(id, grid, boundary, now_k) -> bool` | refused unless a repeat chain has `start ≤ now_k`, is not stopped, and no switch is pending (Q10); sets `pending` (not yet installed); `grid` is the replaced repeat's `(origin, period)` (5g) | both (5d) |
| `install(id, cursor_k, moves) -> Option<i64>` | `cursor_k ≤ boundary`: installed at `boundary`; `cursor_k > boundary` and `moves` (SendAsapAndFlag, 5g): moved to `repeat_boundary(grid, cursor_k)`, installed there (the chosen boundary is kept in the row); otherwise refused, pending dropped | UHD owner at the switch command (5d-U, 5g); Mock at receipt (`moves = false`, `cursor = now_k`, always installed) |
| `advance(now_k)` | at `now_k ≥ boundary` of an installed pending switch: the old repeat's chain ends at `boundary` (`front = boundary`, `join = false`), a new repeat chain starts at `boundary` with origin `boundary` and its own period; `pending = None` | both, each step (5d) |

- **Overlap refusals** become `radio.COMMAND_REJECTED` with the action's id on the Mock (the existing path, `lib.rs:1686-1689`) and on UHD (`command_rejected`, `control.rs` reject path). A refusal after receipt (S12, a stalled host) is asynchronous (Q8).
- **Pending and the device clock (Sol 5).** A switch is pending from its acceptance until `advance` applies it at `now_k ≥ boundary`, on both Providers. Its boundary is set at receipt; the owner moves it only under a SendAsapAndFlag late policy when its cursor has passed it (UR-38, 5g), and a Mock never does. On UHD the switch must also be installed by the owner (`install`) before the owner hands any sample at or past the boundary: installation and refusal are decided at the install, so the record never applies a switch the device did not take.
- **Stop with a pending switch (Sol 5).** `stop(t, stop_k)`: if `pending.boundary ≥ stop_k` the switch is cancelled (outcome `cancelled`). If `boundary < stop_k` the switch has taken effect and is applied before the Stop truncates the new repeat (samples already handed are not recalled; RM-16). The reading of the ruling's "`b ≥ s + W'`" as `b ≥ stop_k(s)` is INFERRED from the ruling's text (the bound already contains `s` and `W`); §10 asks the orchestrator to confirm.

### 1.3 The receipt rule (both Providers, receipt order; replaces rev 1 §1.2's one-sided table)

`accept(start, len, now, join_by)` with candidate region `R = [start, f)`, `now` the receipt in root ticks:

1. `f = start + max(len, 4) + 1` for a standalone head of at most 32 samples; `f = start + len + 2` for a head longer than 32 and for a joined piece; `f = MAX` for a repeat.
2. **Join.** If a chain has `join`, its last end is exactly `start`, and **[M1]** `now ≤ join_by(start)` (the receipt is at or before `J(e)`, `e = start`), the candidate joins it. The chain's own region is excused for this candidate: `R` is tested against every other chain and the region only. The chain's `end` becomes `start + len`, `last` becomes `start`, its front becomes `f`. A repeat never joins.
3. **Otherwise** a new chain is opened. A late join (`now > J(e)`) takes this path and is refused by step 4, since the joined chain's front `e + 2` is past `e`.
4. **Refused** iff `R` meets another chain's region `[c.start, c.front)` or the region `[region.start, region.front)`: `start < c.front && c.start < f`. This is two-sided (C1) and half-open, so back-to-back bookings are accepted (row 08).

Receipt order decides: of two overlapping bookings, the one received second is refused, on both Providers, in both orders (Q1). A continuation is not an overlap, so a join is never refused. A candidate that would fill a gap between two chains never joins either (its `f` exceeds the later chain's start), so there is no two-sided join (INFERRED, §10).

5. **[5h, rev 17, D70, D71] The hand-over horizon.** `accept(start, len, now, join_by, horizon)` and `first_free(k, joins, now, join_by, horizon)` take `horizon = handover_horizon(now, W) = at_or_after(now + W)`, `W = radio.timing.tx_in_flight_ns` in samples (the owner's `window_cut`, `tx.rs:443-444`). A chain `c` may already be on the device at `now` iff `Bookings::may_be_handed(c, now, horizon, join_by)`: `c.start < horizon` and no chain `x` with `x.join`, `x.end ≤ c.start` and `now ≤ join_by(x.end)`. Step 5 refuses a candidate that starts before a handable `c` (`start < c.start`), with `COMMAND_REJECTED` naming its id; `first_free` moves a start that lies before a handable `c` to `c.front`. A booking after a handable chain is judged by steps 1–4 alone.

**[M1, R4-4] The receipt at the deadline.** A join is decided by the record and the `now` the booking carries: `now ≤ J(e)` at `J(e)` is accepted, `now = J(e) + 1` tick is refused. The owner's close is `close(e)` under the streams lock (§3), which clears the join; a booking whose `now` was read before `J(e)` but whose lock is taken after the close finds no join and is refused by step 4. A booking received after `J(e)` that would have continued the chain is therefore refused at receipt by step 4 (its front `e + 2` meets its start `e`), even when its own start is still on time. §2 S4b is the Provider test of that case; the 148 µs case of §3 is a different one (C booked early at `e+2`, played on time after the close).

**[m7]** The device fronts (`+5`, `+1`, `+2`, `+3`) are the device's close shapes and apply on every profile, `ideal` included. Today `short_burst_floor` already applies on every profile (`lib.rs:318`, VERIFIED). `ideal` has lead 0 and window 0 (`channels.rs:352`, VERIFIED), so only its lead and its `stop_k` window differ.

| Earlier chain | Refused for a later booking starting in | Accepted from | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| standalone 1–3 samples at `s` | `[s, s+5)` | `s+5` | padded first send: UR-22 (`design/18-uhd-radio.md:250`), `tx.rs:634,749` (VERIFIED); the `+1` after the pad is INFERRED (no bench row starts at `s+5`) |
| standalone 4–32 samples, end `e` | `[s, e+1)` | `e+1` | `FakeDevice` close refusal `t <= cursor` (`device.rs:689`, VERIFIED) with `E = e`; INFERRED on hardware (Q3) |
| head longer than 32, end `e`, not continued | `[s, e+2)`, except a join at exactly `e` | `e+2` | S3 bench, both boards (VERIFIED, 2026-11-06): `end+1` late, `end+2` on time |
| repeat at `s` | `[s, ∞)` | the Stop bound `stop_k` after a Stop | §0 and the Stop rows (VERIFIED for the window's size, §11 for `+3`) |
| **[M2, R4-2]** a Stop at `t` that cancels a booking | `[t, stop_k)` (the region, hulled with an earlier one) | `stop_k` | §1.2 `halt`; a kept chain's front is capped at `stop_k` (R4-1): its span beyond the bound is not reserved, and its handed span is not recalled (RM-16) |
| any chain, candidate starting **before** it and running into it | the candidate's `[start, f)` meets `[c.start, c.front)` | — | C1: the rule in §1.3 step 4 (INFERRED from the rule; no bench case) |
| **[5h, rev 17, D71]** a chain that may already be handed (`c.start < horizon`, no open join before it), a candidate starting before it | the candidate's start is before `c.start` (refused at receipt, not a region test) | the candidate's start at or after `c.start` | D70, owner decision 2026-11-07; the horizon is `at_or_after(now + W)` (`tx.rs:443-444`) |

### 1.4 What the record replaces (both Providers)

- **UHD.** The identical-start insert (`control.rs:494`); the booking-time SendAsap bound and its held check (`control.rs:472-479`); the owner's overlap-by-dispatch: `preempt` at `tx.rs:435,453` and `:1020`; the owner's immediate close at another held burst (`tx.rs:457-460`) and its deadline (`tx.rs:463-466`, comparison changed in §3); `Busy` (`staging.rs:70-107`, its call `control.rs:498`, its Stop `control.rs:315`); the start set `core.held` (`control.rs:475, 494`; `tx.rs:981-990`) if its readers are only overlap tests (5b-2, checked by grep then).
- **Mock.** The refusal list (`lib.rs:1677-1685`: identical start, and `start ≤ open.next`); the handed front output of `tx_handed_front` (`lib.rs:1389-1422`, the `+2` at `:1419`, `device_floor` at `:1406`); `short_burst_floor` (`lib.rs:318`); `remember_tx_front` (`lib.rs:1424`) and its callers (`lib.rs:1169-1180`, `:1816`), with `TxFront` (`lib.rs:285`); the late-front call `late_policy_front_outcome` at `lib.rs:1648` (its front is `None` after `first_free`); the preemption tests (§6.1).
- **[m8]** A host-late move or drop of a chain's head leaves its joined pieces as heads on the Module, which may then be closed short (`tx.rs:594`) and a later join on them is late. This is recorded as a host-lateness consequence (the Module was already late by the host's own stall), not re-framed; the record does not change the chain on `drop_piece`. RM-14/UR-23 say so in 5b-3.
- **[Q-6]** `chain.front` on UHD is the device fact (the configure guard `tx.rs:514`, dispatch lateness `tx.rs:972-976`); the record's fronts are booking facts. A stat counts each owner close whose front exceeds the record's front (5b-2b); it is zero in the on-time and parity tests (§4.6).
- **Kept.** `tx_handed_front`'s payload output (Stop's payload cut, `lib.rs:1167`); `handed_end` and `handed_end_before` (`lib.rs:302-308`) and `merge_front` (`lib.rs:310`), which the payload cut uses; `chain.front` on UHD (`tx.rs:114`, configure guard `tx.rs:514`, host lateness `tx.rs:972-976`); `later_bound` (`timeline.rs:339`, callers `tx.rs:973` and the Stop cut `lib.rs:1169`); `late_policy_front_outcome` (`timeline.rs:344`, caller `tx.rs:976`).

## 2. The sequences: now and after

Sequences per Provider are pinned by §4. `B` is booked after `A`, unless marked "received first".

| Seq | Now (Mock / UHD) | After (both Providers, receipt order) |
|---|---|---|
| S1 (#79 original) | A 1 sample at `s`; B at `s+1..s+4`: Mock accepts and moves B to the frontier; UHD accepts and decides late at dispatch | B at `s+1..s+4` refused at receipt; `s+5` accepted, on time |
| S2 | A of 4, 10, 32 at `s`; B at `s+len`: Mock moves to `end+2`; UHD late at dispatch | B at `end` refused; B at `end+1` accepted, on time |
| S3 | A of 40 at `s`; B at `s+n`, `n = 1..39`: Mock moves after the frontier; UHD preempts the held burst | every B refused; A's samples unchanged (nothing cut) |
| S4 | A of 40 at `s`; B at `s+40`: Mock moves it to the frontier; UHD continues (VERIFIED for the device) | B at `s+40` joins A (one device burst); B at `s+41` refused; B at `s+42` accepted |
| S4b (Sol 1) **[R4-5]** | A of 40 at `s` fully handed; C at `s+100` booked early; then B at `s+40`, received at or before `J(s+40)` | B joins A (continuation, no end-of-burst between); C plays on time at `s+100`. Today C's booking makes the owner close A at once (`tx.rs:457-460`), so B is late |
| S4c (Sol 2) | A of 40 at `s`; B of 10 at `s+40` (joins); C at `s+51` (B's end + 1) | C refused (B's device burst still needs B's end + 2); C at `s+52` accepted, on time (1651's case with its real assertions) |
| S5 | S1–S4, S4b, S4c with the second booking **received first** | the one received second is refused where regions meet, on both Providers, whichever booking it is: e.g. B(40) at `s+40` received first, then A(40) at `s`: A refused (its region `[s, s+42)` meets B's) |
| S6 (SendAsap) | booking late by receipt: Mock to `later_bound(ordinary, front)`; UHD to `now + lead` | moved to `first_free(ordinary_k)`; if the moved region meets a later booking: refused with one `TIME_ERROR{refused}`; if free: accepted with one `TIME_ERROR{late, send_asap}` |
| S6d (Sol 3, dispatch) | a head delayed at dispatch past its first send: UHD moves it (`dispatch_start`, `tx.rs:968-996`); the identical-start check is the only test | the moved region meets a later booking: the moved burst is refused with `TIME_ERROR{refused}`, never preempts, never moves the later booking; its payload end still counts for stage 4 (C6). Sol 3's case: A `[20,40)` ms, B at 50 ms, A's first dispatch at 40 ms moves it to `[42,62)` ms |
| S7 (repeat) | a plain repeat or burst while a repeat plays: Mock accepts after the frontier; UHD preempts | refused with its id. After a Stop at `t`: refused before `stop_k(t)`, accepted from it |
| S8 switch, same length | — | the new waveform starts at the boundary; the period stays locked; the record fields are set |
| S9 switch, different length | — | starts at the boundary; the new period is the new length; the old repeat is cut at the boundary |
| S10 | a second pending switch; a switch on a stopped stream, on a finite burst, or on a repeat booked but not started (Q10) | refused with its id; the first pending switch still applies |
| S11 | a Stop, loss, `Provider::stop` or F8 recovery between the switch and its boundary | cancelled when `boundary ≥ stop_k`; otherwise applied and then cut (§1.2); outcome recorded; no sample of the new waveform is sent after a cancellation |
| S12 (UHD) | host stall: the cursor is past the boundary when the owner installs | **[5g]** SendAsapAndFlag: moved to the first boundary of the replaced repeat's grid at or after the cursor, installed there, one `TIME_ERROR { send_asap, action }`, outcome `applied`, the row keeps both boundaries (`boundary`, `moved_to`). DropAndFlag: refused with its id (outcome `refused_past_boundary`); the old repeat keeps playing unchanged |
| S13 (#79's value case) **[m5]** | one-sample A of `s` (padded device end `s+4`); B of 100 samples at `s+1` under `DropAndFlag`, received at `s+1`; a value received at `s+5`; a value received at `s+1` and at `s` | B refused at receipt on both (S1), and a booking refused at receipt adds no value end (C6 applies only to bookings accepted and later cancelled or refused at dispatch). The value at `s+5` is staged on both; a value at `s+1` (payload end `s+1 ≤ s+1`) is staged on both; `s` is refused on both (`TxBusy` until `s+1`). Pinned in **5b-2** (not 5a: today the Providers disagree on B, so 5a cannot pin one answer) |
| S14 (Python) | `switch(x)`; a second plain `repeat` on a playing stream | `switch` replaces; the second `repeat` is refused by the Provider at booking, and Python raises `Rejected` from the submit reply (Q8) |
| S15 (parity) | S1–S7, S4b, S4c, S13 on `parity.rs`, both receipt orders, inside and outside the window | both Providers answer alike |

S13 is spelled out as the ruling asks. Its text is #79's comment as the orchestrator summarized it on 2026-11-07; the comment itself is not read in this revision (§11).

## 3. The owner's change at the held-tail close (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs:442-466`)

Today (VERIFIED, `tx.rs:442-466`):

- `Some(_) => { self.end_open(true); return true; }` (`tx.rs:457-460`): another burst held next (not the continuation) ends the device burst at once. That is the premature close Sol 1 shows: C is booked early, A is closed, and B at A's end arrives too late.
- `None if clock.instant(c) - now <= self.core.ticks(DEVICE_LEAD_NS) => { self.end_open(true); return true; }` (`tx.rs:463-466`): closes at the deadline, with `<=`.
- `None => return false` (`tx.rs:467`): waits.

After 5b-2b (the held-tail close):

- **[M1, R4-4]** Both branches close only when the record says no booking can continue the chain: `!continuation_open(e, now)` (§1.2), with `now` read under the streams lock the owner already takes (`streams`), in the same critical section as the record read. When it closes, the owner calls `close(e)` in that same section, so a join received after the close is refused whatever `now` it carries. A piece joined at `e` is never closed at `e`; the owner waits for that piece's `Held`.
- **[M1, R4-m2] The close instant.** The owner closes at the first step at which `now > J(e) = instant(e) − (lead + allowance)`, or earlier if the record allows it. There is no idle-sleep cap: the idle sleep is `IDLE` = 200 µs (`tx.rs:286-288`, `:218`), so the close lands at most one `IDLE` after `J(e)`, still 3 ms before the device lead's deadline `instant(e) − lead` (the allowance). A booking received after `J(e)` is refused (§1.3), so the late close cannot admit one.
- Otherwise the owner returns `false` and waits. A held later burst is not dispatched from there: the `chain.next()` block returns before the held-burst dispatch (`tx.rs:448-468` precedes `tx.rs:470`), so C's first send waits for the close.
- **[R4-5]** There is one join deadline, `J(e)`, on every path (S4b, row 58, this section). A booking received at or before `J(e)` finds the chain open; a booking received after it does not (§1.3). The old `<=` in `tx.rs:463` closed one step early for a booking that started exactly at the deadline (`N47S5-26`, withdrawn in rev 4).
- **[M3]** `h <= next` (`tx.rs:433-438`) and `k < c` (`tx.rs:452-455`) call `preempt`. The `k < c` branch goes in 5c, since overlap refusal makes it unreachable. The `h <= next` branch is **reduced, not deleted**: at `cursor == b` the switch's `Held` has `h == next` and must continue the device burst (`end_open(false)`, then continue). Today it reaches `preempt` (`tx.rs:433-436`, `tx.rs:1064-1066`), and deleting it would make `send` compute `wanted = min(…, b − next) = 0` and trip `debug_assert!(count > 0)` (`tx.rs:579-582`), which fails `ur_21_s8_switch_at_the_exact_handed_cursor_installs` (5d-U). The `h < next` re-decision is what goes (C2 is corrected: the `=` case keeps its path).
- **[M1, Q-6]** A stat counts each owner close whose front exceeds the record's front for the same chain; it must be zero in the on-time and parity tests (§4.6).

A booking received within host jitter of the close is host lateness (Sol 1), not a record error.

The M1 case on the owner's close is pinned by §4.2 (`ur_21_m1_…`). The 148 µs case: C booked early at the chain front `e+2` plays on time on UHD after a held tail closed at `J(e)` with the manual clock stepped 150 µs past the deadline. Today's code closes as soon as C is held (`tx.rs:457-460`), so it never produces that case; the plan introduces it and fixes it in 5b-2b.

---

## 6. Removal and re-anchoring inventory (row by row, Sol 9)

Method: each test or row named for removal was read against its `mutations.json` entry (`old` text, `filter`) and against the code it exercises, in this revision (VERIFIED where a line is cited). Each row or test has exactly one disposition: **DELETE** (with the sub-step that removes it and its evidence), **REWRITE** (the same test name or a new one, with a killing row), **RE-ANCHOR** (the row stays, its `old` text changes), or **KEEP**.

### 6.3 The N47S4 family and the dead helpers

- **No N47S4 row is deleted.** The family (`N47S4-01…-56`, 64 rows) is stage 4's value rule, the owner's configure and mark rules, and the Python EA-16 rules. Rev 1 named `N47S4-03…-53` as preemption; that was wrong (C3). `N47S4-39` (clock ending at its origin) and `N47S4-53` (Python channel targeting) keep their tests (`mr_25_a_transmit_clock_ends_at_its_origin…`, `session.py`'s channel test).
- **Re-anchored**: §6.2 lists the 18 rows whose text names `Busy`, `busy`, `until`, `in_flight` or `stageable`.
- **Dead helpers (Q12).** `late_policy_front_outcome` (`timeline.rs:344`) keeps its caller `tx.rs:976` after 5c (host lateness, `dispatch_start`); the Mock's caller `lib.rs:1648` goes in 5b-2. Result: not deleted. `later_bound` (`timeline.rs:339`) keeps `tx.rs:973` and the Mock's Stop cut `lib.rs:1169`. Result: not deleted. `short_burst_floor` (`lib.rs:318`) and `remember_tx_front` (`lib.rs:1424`) lose their callers in 5b-2 and are deleted with them. The 5c report states each count.

## 7. Spec and KNOWN_LIMITATIONS texts that change

Accepted specs are normative text only, one topic per rule, each ending in its `*Test:*` line (AGENTS §2). `plan/maintenance/tools/rules.py` must pass with `--base c41530f`. New rules take the next free IDs in each spec (checked in the sub-step that adds them). No ID is renumbered or reused; withdrawn rules keep a one-line stub.

| RM-14 | a SendAsap move is to `first_free(ordinary_k)` and the moved region is judged as at receipt (S6); a dispatch move that meets a later booking is refused with `TIME_ERROR` (Sol 3) | 5b-2, 5b-3 |
| RM-15 | the receipt rule of §1.3 (two-sided overlap; join with its receipt deadline `J(e)` **[M1]**; the held-tail and standalone fronts), and the repeat boundary `repeat_boundary` | **[R4-m6]** 5b-2a (text and wiring, one commit) |
| RM-16 | a repeat occupies `[start, ∞)` until a Stop; a Stop cuts the open repeat at `stop_k`, adds the region `[t, stop_k)` **[M2]** and never lowers a finite front; cancellation of bookings starting at or after the Stop; the pending switch's cancellation rule (Sol 5) | 5b-2a (Stop region), 5d (switch) |

## 8. Sub-steps (each leaves the tree green and fits one run; no wall-clock test)

- **[rev 33] Note 47 stage 5, fix round 8 (the Opus re-check of round 7: Minor A, the unverified drop at dispatch, Minor B; brief `brief-fix8.md`, findings `opus-fix7-recheck.md`; implementer, 2026-11-07; branch `work/n47s5`, uncommitted).** One line per item; probes in `probes-fix8/summary.txt`. Each sequence was written first as a test on the manual clock and run on the round-7 code; "failed before" is that run.
  - **Minor A (the held switch of an abandoned repeat).** `abandon` (`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/src/provider/tx.rs`) removes every held switch at or after the handed cursor whenever the abandoned burst is a repeat, whatever `switched` says, through the new `Tx::cancel_held_switches`; `cancel_switch_from` runs only for a repeat. The removal is no longer gated on `switched`, so the open-repeat path is covered too (a superset of the brief's held-switch case; recorded). The record's entry for a switch it already applied is settled cancelled by the new `Bookings::cancel_applied` (`crates/ezsdr-radio/src/staging.rs`), so the row does not read `applied`. Test `ur_21_s4_a_switch_joined_then_abandoned_late_is_cancelled_not_applied` (both late policies): failed before: yes (at the held-burst assertion: the held switch was still in the held set). Rows N47S5-152 (`cancel_applied` never matches, killer that test) KILLED; N47S5-154 (the held removal not called, killer that test) KILLED; N47S5-155 (`>=` → `>` in the held filter, killer that test) KILLED.
  - **Unverified item (a late repeat dropped at dispatch).** Verified by test: the DropAndFlag drop at dispatch (`dispatch_start`, `tx.rs`) called no `drop_piece`, so the endless chain stayed in the record. Test `ur_21_s4_a_late_repeat_dropped_at_dispatch_leaves_the_record`: the repeat is on time at receipt (device at 0) and late at dispatch (device at 3 ms); a finite burst on time at 20 000 is refused by the leftover chain. **failed before: yes** (`UR-21: the burst overlaps an accepted booking`). The drop now calls `drop_piece` as the other refusals do (`tx.rs`). Note: a repeat that is late at receipt is dropped by the Control at receipt and never reaches the record, so the test uses a booking that is on time at receipt. Row N47S5-156 (the `drop_piece` removed, killer that test) KILLED.
  - **Minor B (a finite burst's abandon cancels a switch whose repeat has not started).** Option chosen: local, option 1. The abandon cancels a pending switch only when the abandoned burst is a repeat, so a finite burst's abandon leaves it pending. A repeat dropped at dispatch cancels its pending switch (`Bookings::cancel_switch` now returns whether one was pending) and the held switch goes with it (`cancel_held_switches`). A late-moved repeat keeps its switch, as the Opus note asks. Test `ur_21_s4_a_finite_abandon_keeps_the_switch_of_the_repeat_it_has_not_started`: the SendAsap variant expects the switch `applied` and the new waveform sent at its boundary; the DropAndFlag variant expects `cancelled` and nothing sent. **failed before: yes** for the SendAsap variant (`cancelled with the repeat it replaces`: the finite abandon cancelled it); the DropAndFlag variant **failed before: no** (by reading, not run on round 7: the old abandon cancelled the switch in this sequence too; the variant pins the new drop branch, killed by rows N47S5-157 and -158). Row N47S5-153 (the repeat guard removed, killer the Minor B test) KILLED; N47S5-157 (no `cancel_switch` on a dropped repeat, killer the Minor B test) KILLED; N47S5-158 (the held switch not removed on a dropped repeat, killer the Minor B test) KILLED.
  - **Rows changed or retired.** N47S5-148 and -149 mutated the removed `if switched` block and its `>=` filter; their mutants are the same programs as N47S5-154 and -155, so they are removed (ids not reused). N47S5-150 (the abandon cancels only while a burst is open) was re-anchored, probed NOT KILLED (`probes-fix8/summary.txt`): after the change its guarded condition has no observable effect (a held switch at or after the cursor is removed and settled cancelled in the same abandon, and the record's stop region from the cut is the one the repeat's front already gives), so it is an equivalent mutant on this profile (INFERRED from the reading of `halt`; the probe result is measured), and the row is removed as obsolete. P710 (`dispatch_start` now takes `&mut self`) re-anchored to the same program, re-probed KILLED.
  - **Spec texts.** RM-37 (`design/07-radio-model.md`): the cancellation sentence adds the dispatch drop and says a finite burst's abandon leaves a pending switch; its Test line names the two new tests; a Changes row. UR-38 (`design/18-uhd-radio.md`): a continuation sentence (an abandon of the repeat at the handed cursor or its drop at dispatch cancels it; a finite burst's abandon does not); a Changes row. KNOWN_LIMITATIONS unchanged: the fix is local (Minor B option 1), so no entry was added. `rules.py --base HEAD` → 0.
  - **Residual (not changed, not tested; for the orchestrator).** A switch the record has applied, whose held burst is then dropped at dispatch (a host stall past its boundary before the owner's first turn; INFERRED, not reached in any test), keeps its `applied` row: its region is removed by `drop_piece`, but `cancel_applied` is not called on the drop path. `cancel_switch_from` in `abandon` is largely redundant with `cancel_held_switches` now (INFERRED from the reading of `halt`; the Stop cut region's effect is the same): a candidate for the Phase-8-end review.

## 9. Failure scenarios (mine; each has a test)

1. **Receipt order with a join.** B(40) at `s+40` received first, then A(40) at `s`: A refused (its region meets B). A first: B joins. Both are pinned (S5, `mr_16_s5_receipt_order_both_orders`, killer 07).
2. **A host late at dispatch after acceptance.** A booking accepted at receipt is not refused later. Only a dispatch-time move that meets a later booking is refused (`ur_23_s3_…`, killer 31). Host lateness alone never drops an accepted booking that is still on time.
3. **Two Stops.** The first Stop's tail `[t1, stop_k(t1))`; a second Stop at `t2 < stop_k(t1)` must not extend it (`mr_25_s7_second_stop_does_not_extend_the_tail`, killer 09).
4. **Stop at the boundary sample.** Stop at `t = boundary`: the switch is cancelled (`boundary ≥ stop_k` if the stop is later than the boundary, otherwise applied first, §1.2) and no sample of the new waveform is sent (`mr_25_s11_stop_at_boundary_cancels_switch`, killer 36).
5. **Switch chain.** Switch 1 applies at `b1`; switch 2 accepted after `b1` uses `b1` as origin with its own length (the applied boundary, not the receipt). Test: `mr_16_s9_…` extended (killer 33).
6. **Switch cut of a short first period.** The old repeat's cut must leave a legal closed send (UR-22). Test: `ur_23_switch_cut_reuses_held_cut_path` (killer 41 family; a legal close is a precondition).
7. **Python: second repeat after a switch.** The second `repeat` is refused by the Provider and raises `Rejected` from the reply; `switch` replaces (`test_ea_16_second_plain_repeat…`, killer 48).
8. **A booking just before an open repeat.** `[start, ∞)` overlaps from `start`; a booking before it is accepted when its own region ends before `start` (its padded front, `start − 10` with 1 sample ends at `start − 5`). A booking at `start − 1` whose padded front reaches `start` is refused by the two-sided rule (C1), not accepted (`rm_16_repeat_occupies_until_a_stop`, killer 11).
9. **Cancelled bookings keep counting for values (C6).** A Stop cancels a burst at `t`; a value received before the cancelled end and after the stop bound is refused as today (`rm_21_busy_is_max_accepted_end`, killer 01; `ur_21_a_refused_burst_stays_counted_until_its_booked_end`, N47S4-13b).

## 10. Open questions for the orchestrator (with my recommendation)

- **Q-1. Two-sided refusal (C1).** Rev 1's table refused only starts inside `[s, a)`; this rule also refuses a candidate that starts before an accepted booking and runs into it. Recommendation: the two-sided rule (it is what "the one received second is refused" means, Q1). It changes one rev 1 table row, so the orchestrator should confirm it before 5b-2.
- **Q-2. The reading of "`b ≥ s + W'`" (Sol 5).** Recommendation: `boundary ≥ stop_k(s)`, the bound already contains `s` and `W`. Confirm before 5d. **[rev 12]** Implemented as written in 5d-M (`halt`: `b < stop` applied, `b ≥ stop` cancelled). It conflicts with S11's "Stop at the boundary cancels" (D42): the orchestrator should confirm the rule, and S11's expectation changes to "applied, cut at the horizon" if it holds.
- **Q-3. Delete the hardware preemption sweep** (`hw_b8_preemption`, `hardware.rs:650`; `measurement.rs` ~2099–2123; `iii.rs:110, 556`). Recommendation: delete in 5c. Its bench rows stay as evidence in `bench-results.md`.
- **Q-4. Bench items for the next bench (not gating; Q3, Q4 as the ruling asks).** (a) `end+1` after a 4–32 sample close on two boards at 1 and 2 MS/s, with the attenuators confirmed (handoff §2): the Mock/record fronts are the Module's code, so the invariant holds whichever way the bench answers; (b) the gap-2 no-tail close (`stop_k` `+3`, the 2026-10-27 empty-EOB row); (c) the `+5` after a 1–3 sample burst (no bench row starts at `s+5`); (d) the switch's boundary on hardware (a repeat switch, boundary at receipt + W + 2 ms); (e) the 10 ms window against the 2026-11-05 profile row (3 ms, not used); **(f) [Q-4, M1]** C at the front `e+2` after a held tail closed at `J(e)` (the 148 µs case on the bench); **(g) [Q-4]** a switch at `b` (the continuation on hardware, no EOB at `b`). Recommendation: one bench session, listed for the owner, not run in this stage.
- **[rev 3] Resolved:** Q-1 (two-sided, adopted), Q-2 (`b ≥ stop_k(s)`, adopted; m4 caveat), Q-3 (delete in 5c), Q-5 (prune in 5b-2a), Q-6 (keep `chain.front`; stat in 5b-2b), Q-7 (one lead passed in; `J(e)` uses lead + allowance). Recommendation of Q-4 unchanged.
- **[m6] Host timing** (not a question): the Stop's `t` is control's `now`, and the owner may hand one more step before it drains `TxCmd::Stop`. Its front can pass `stop_k` by one step's duration. Named in RM-16 and UR-23 as host timing; the parity tests at `stop_k` use the manual clock.
- **Q-5. Pruning the record.** Chains and `dropped_ends` grow with the Run's bookings (today's `until` is one scalar). Recommendation: prune pieces whose front is at or before the device's position and whose ends are at or before the newest value receipt, in 5f; not before (rows would need a test for each prune).
- **Q-6. Two fronts on UHD (C7).** `chain.front` (owner, handed) and the record's fronts (booking) can disagree after a host stall. Recommendation: keep `chain.front` only for the configure guard and host lateness (the ruling), and give dispatch a single source (`move_start` on the record), in 5b-3 if the grep shows no other reader; otherwise 5f.
- **Q-7. The lead that decides lateness (parity hazard).** The booking decision uses 2 ms on both Providers: UHD `DEVICE_LEAD_NS` (`control.rs:450`, VERIFIED) and Mock's profile command lead (`lib.rs:1633`, 2 ms in the x3xx Mock profile, VERIFIED). The UHD profile declares a 5 ms minimum command lead (`profile.rs:21`: device lead 2 ms plus delivery allowance 3 ms, VERIFIED). Recommendation: `ordinary_k` takes the decision lead explicitly (2 ms, passed in, as Q5 does for the switch) on both, and the 5 ms declared value is recorded as a parity hazard for the TimingEnvelope, not changed in stage 5.
